Re Loy's eneroaelunenf on the publie road at Castletown,
the consideration of which was adjourned from last meeting—
The County Surveyor, in reply to the Chairman, stated that
the porch erected by Mr Loy at Castletown inside limit
prescribed by the Act was not yet removed. The Council
unanimously ordered that the Solicitor get instructions to
take proceedings forthwith at the Petty Sessions Court to have
the obstruction removed.
Re Haynestown Criminal Injury—The Solicitor also got
instructions not to appeal in this case. The amount of com-
pensation awarded at Quarter Sessions was C26.
Owing to tho Election for North Louth falling on the samo
day as tho County Council Meetinct, all other business was
adjourned until the next Meeting of the Council on the 24th
day of February, 1910.

ESTIMATE OF RATES.
The Secretary submitted his Estimate of Rates for the
ensuing year, which was fully considered, and on ithe motion
Of Mr Goodman; seconded by Mr Wynne, it was resolved—That
We approve of same and recommend its adoption by tho County
Council, and that a printed copy be furnishcd to each member
Of the Council, with an explanatory Table settin+ out in detail
the difference between this year's and: last year s Estimates.
PREPARING THE RATE BOOKS, ETC, ENSUING
YEAR.
Tho Cornmittee, after due deliberation, were of opinion thnt
Under the present system the work is most unsatisfactorily
done and, the expenses incurred (C180) excessive. They, there-
fore, recommend' that the Secretary be requested to undertake
this work in future at a fixed sum to be determined by tho
Council.
EXECUTIVE COMMITTEE.
Minutes of Meeting held on, Monday, 14th February, 1910.
Tho' Secretary submitted the Veterinary Inspeetors' Monthly
Roports, etc.
Mr Maxwell reported no cases of Constagious Disease in his
District.
Mr Donnelly reported 8 sheep at Drogheda affected with
Seal). The sheep were dippe€i twice during the month, and
are now free from Scab. Gates, rubbing-posts and premises
disinfected, and all loose wool collected and burned.
Mr Clinton reports that two outbreaks of Sheep Scab
in his district—one at Mountpleasant and the other
at Dundalk. The necessary steps for stamping out the disease
were immediately taken and both flocks of sheep were dipped,
etc. A supposed case of rabies in a dog at Knockbridge was
reported. T immediately visited the place and forwarded the
animal's head for examination,. Personally I do not believe

LOUDEN v. EASTWOOD ESTATE, IN CHANCERY.
Proposed bv J T Dolan, Esq, M A (Ardee)
Seconded by P Hughes, Esq, J P, Chairman, and
Resolved—That we, the Members of the Louth County
Ouncil
, ,being strongly of opinion that this case was one of
eXtrerne •urgency, unanimously resolved on the 28th May last
to request the Estates Commissioners to reinstate this tenant in
his former holding at Castletown, in this county, before the
expiration of the Evicted Tenants (Ireland) Act, 1907, and as
nothing definite appears to have yet been done towards pur-
chase of the farm from the Court of Chancery, and as the said
Act expires at May next, we now ask the Leader Of the United.
Irish Parliamentary Party to bring the matter before Parlia-
ment at the earliest opportune moment, so as to ascertain the
cause of the unnecessarv delay in dealing with this case ; that
a statement, of the true facts of the case be forwarded to each
member of the Party, the Chief Secretary for Ireland, tho
Estates Commissioners, and the Court of Chancery.
Unanimously. adopted.

Louth County Council Orrice,
Dundalk, 16th February, 1910.
To the Chairman and Members of the County Council.
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
I beg to present the Estimate of Rates for General and'
Separate Charges for the Financial Year commencing on 1st
April, 1910. The subjoined tables for the greater part explain
themselves, and it is only necessary that I should make a short
statement in explanation Of the slight increases proposed.
As to County Charges, the amount you require to malw
provision for is practically the same as last yea r. except that
the portion chargeable to Urban Districts and Rural) is some—
what Ia rger, and that chargeable to Rura Districts is less.
The different items to which Urban Districts contribute will De-
furnished to tho Clerks of these Districts with your Demand.
As to Union Charges. The Demand furnished bv the Guard-
ians of Ardee No 1 Union is increased by C73. 'In Drogheda
there is a decrease ofC'200 and in Dundalk decrease of C711.
As to Rural Districts. in Ardee the Demand from the Rural'
District Council is the same as last year. In Drogheda there is.
a decrease of Ell—too small to affect a rate—and in Dundalk
7
yon are required to provide more than last year.
•s
regards the other items of exoenditure applicable to Rnral Dis-
Charges—that is, Road Works, Agricultural and Technical
Instruction, Education Act Expenses; Collectors' K'emuneration
and irrecoverable arrears of Rate, I have ascertained that the
amount estimated for last vear (taking these neads of account
collectively) was considerably less than it should have been,
judging by actual expenditure, and this is more appa rent in
D ufidalk than in either of the two Rural Districts; also, for
next' year, the County Surveyor anticipates au increase in
respect of Road Work of CIOO each in Drogheda and Dundalk
Rural Districts.
As regards receipts applicable to Rural District Charges (and
Unions), I find the amount estimated last year was excessive,
and have reclueocl this figure in the present Estimate by C300
spread over the three contributory areas.
It is of course, understood that an obligation rests on the
Councii to provide funds in advanee for the various Board of
Guardians and Rural District Councils in the- County, and for
Richmond District I ,uuatie Asylum. For this purpose it is
necessary that the Cryunt.y Treasurer should nave at his dis-
PO.sal a large credit balance, and in my opimon that balance
should be somewhat la rger than it is at present. Not infre-
quently these payments have Io 'be delayed, to the ineon-
V enienee of the-public bodies concerned, and in this eonnection
the Transfers ot'lered at last Quarterly Meeting to Guardians,
District Councils and Richmond Asvlum have beery delft •ced for
of funds, although in some instances urgently called for.
The credit required for these advance payments stands,
Of COurse, to the credit of the District from which it was levied,
and on tho 31st March, (our last completed year) the
amount, standing to credit of Dundalk Rural District Charges
Was only C930, as compared with C3,176 on the 31st March,
1906. Under this head (i.e., District Charges) the Estimate
has been slightly inflated, and more so in Dundalk than in
either of tho two Rural Districts. On the whole Estimate the
net increase is 2d in the e in Ardee and Drogheda Districts,
and 4d in the C in Dundalk.
In my opinion the amount you are asked Co provide is no
more than is absolutely needed for the requirements of the
Gunty.
The Table of Separate Charges (annexed) esplains itself, and
contains only the figures necessary to meet each separate
charge.
I am, Gentlemen,
Your obedient servant,
T. r. FILGATE,
Secretary-

EDUCATIONAL GRANTS.
Proposed by Mr Edward Goodman,
Seconded by Mr Michael (Y Meara, J P, and
Revsolvecl—'l'hat we, tile County Council of Louth, hereby de-
Clare that in our opinion the pfesent unsatisfactory system of
Irish Plimary Education is rnainly due to the parsimony of the
British Treasury, which, while overtaxing this country to the
extent of some millions yearly, has for a number of years in
defiance of justice and constitutional rights, withheld from this
country an average annual grant of nearly half a million ster-
ling. (2) That as for the efficiency Of Irish Primary Education,
an essential element cf National progress, it is absolutely ne-
cessary that adequate financial provision should be for
Schools, Pupils, and Teachers, we hold that as an instalment
of justice to thls country, which has hitherto been, in compari-
son with Engl,nncl and Scotland, educationally stayv€d, tho
Treasury sh0i11cl immediately provide for Irish Education in-
creased on liberal a scale ns are given for tho educa-
tion Che people of Scotiand. (3) That the increased grants
should be allocated for the following purposes To improve
the Teachers' Pension Scheme, which. to put it mildly, is a dis-
graee to the British Government. (b) To provide for the pro-
per equipment, sanitation, heating, and staffing of the Na-
tional Schools, and for supplies of hooks and requisites to
Scholars.
(e) To provide the small sunn required to give se-
cnrity of tenure to Assistant Teachers, by allowing a margin
between the averages which warrant their appointment, and
those which compel tlieir dismissal. (d) To abolish the Stand-
ard Numbers which debar form promotion to the higher grades
hundreds of Teachers who are eminently entitled to it by long
and efficient service.
(e) To make provisions for the promotion
7
Of all undergraded Teachers to the grade corresponding to
their classification. (f) To provide for a generous system of
Scholarships, which will enable pupils of industry and ability in
Primary Schools to go forward to the Secondary Schools, and
thence to the Universities, thus alloting to the children of the
masses equality of opportunity, and placing at the disposal Of
the natiou the b«st available talent.

CORRESPONDENCE.
Dublin Castle, 10th December, 19()9.
Sir,
In reply to your letter of the 29th ultimo, on the
subject of extra Police Expenses for the half year to the 30th
September, 1909, I have to state for the infomation of your
Council, that the County Free Force is maintained out of
funds provided by Parliament: and that no portion of its cost
falls on the county ; no allowance can, therefore, be made to
the county in respect of the absence, under orders of his Ex-
cellency the Lord Lieutenant, of men of the Free Force on de-
tachment duty elsewhere.
N. CHAMBERLAIN,
Col., Inspector-General.
the infortnation of the Louth County Council, copy of an Order
made by their Excellencies directing the deduction of Cö 18s 4d
from su ms payable to the Council from the Local Taxation
(Ireland) Account.

THE CORONATION OATH.
Proposed by Mr. T. C. Macardle, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. M. O'Meara, J.P., and
Resolved—That the Louth County Council* respectfully call
upon the House of Commons to take such steps at tho opening
Of Parliament as may be necessary to promote a measure
deleting from the Coronation Oath those passages which are so
11
hurtful to the Faith and most tender feelings of the Catholic
subjects of his Majesty King George V, not only in Ireland,
but scattered over the Empire, and thus refieve his Majesty
from taking an oath which must obviously be repugnant to his
wide and varied experience of the world, and against which
the great majority of our Protestant fellow-countrymen join
in protesting.

County Surveyor Report on same as under
CLOGHERHEAD PIER.
With reference to the letter No. 4032-10 of the 31st of March,
1910, from thc Secretary, Office of Public Works, Dublin, on
the 19th inst T inspected the Pier. Timber curbs, 6 inches
14
square and 370 feet total length, are fixed along the edge of
the Quay to prevent carts being backed over the edge into the-
sea. During a recent storm these curbs were slightly damaged,
but they are still amply strong for their purpose, as they could
not be broken by several carts. To guard against further•
damage by the sea, I have arranged to have iron 'bars bolted
over the damaged beams.
The cracks in the floor of the Pier may indicate weakness,
but to take up the floor and re-lay it would be absurd. Thew
cracks are one-tenth inch or lexs in width, and they do not
cause inconvenience. I do not think that a ll.vthing would be
gained by coment grouting.
Also these cracks act as flow-
holes, and rough weather the pressure under the floor would
he very considerable.
P. J. LY-NAM,
County Surveyor of Louth.
Proposed by Mr T J Byne, J P,
Seconded by Mr E Goodman, and
Resolved—That a copy of the County Surveyor's Report be
forwarded to the Board of Works.

COUNTY St'l€VEYOR'S REPORT ON ROAD ROLLING.
011 the motion of Mr James Wynne, seconded by Mr James
M 'Court, J P, it was recommended—That the undermentioned
Roport of the County Surveyor on the subject of Road Rolling
18
be adopted, and that the said Report be submitted and read
at the next meeting of the C'Ounty Council :
it
follows :—
COUNTY LOUTH.
Sub-Committee on Steam Rolling, 27th June,
Mr Glairman,
I beg to recommend—
reads as
1910.
(1). That a Steam Roller and Plant be purchased by the
•County Council at a price not, exceeding C680, and that .C32()
be provided for repairs during a period of ten years, provided
that the Rural District Councils adopt Steam Rolling.
(2). That if the Rural Councils adopt Steam Rolling CI,OOO
for the above purpose be borrowed from the Commissioners of
Pnblic Works at 31 per cent—principal and interest to be paid
off •by ten annual payments of C116 lis 7d each, the payments
to be levied off the Ckjunty-at-large, Urban districts excepted.
(3). That the Rural Councils be asked to formulate pro-
posal.s for the working expenses of a Roller.
The working
expenses include the wages oi the engineer, driver, flagman and
forernan spreader, coal and coke, oil, ete, and are estimated at,
22s per day for 240 working days, or C264 per annum. A
Roller could be kept at work if Ardee No 1 Rural District,
would pass C99 for 90 working (lays, Drogheda (Louth) C52 IGs
•Od for 48 clays, and Dundalk C112 'Is Od for days. total,
C2(iI.for 240 days' rolling. rnle•s the Rural District Councils
•agree to pay for working expenses, in proportion to the num-
ber of days rolling done in each district, rolling cannot be
carried on, and the matter must be dropped.
(4). That, if Rolling be adopted by the Rural Councils, a
Stearn Rolling Account 'be opened to show the total working
19
•expenses of the Roller for each year (including half wages for
the three men during stoppages, due to accidents, repairs and
frost), also the number of days' rolling done in each district,
and the amount in proportion charged to each district. This
account to be open to inspection by any ratepayer in the Rural
Districts on payment of a small fee.
(5). That if Rolling be adopted and existing contracts ex-
Pire, the County Surveyor be directed to include in each new
'Contract on principal roads a specified number of days' rolling
one day's rolling to each 40 or cubic yards of broken
stones. The Roller to be supplied to contractors free of charge,
but each contractor to be bound to have sufficient men to spread
the broken stones befoie the Roller quickly, provide six men
to blind and sweep, and a driver and horse for one water cart,
or, if necessary, for two water carts. AIBO the contractor should
coal for tho engine—about 4 cwts per day—and a contrac-
tor should be liable io penalties if he delays the Ikt)ller and
.keeps it Idle.
(G). That contractors not now bound to employ a Roller
be asked to volunteer to u.so it under the conditions as above
(paragraph 5). About one-third of the length of a contract
to be heavily sheeted with broken stones and rolled in each
Year, the wmaining two-thirds to have ruts darned. If a
Sufficient nn Inber of contractors do not volunteer, then the
Purchase of a Roller to be postponed for a year or two, or the
blX)ken stones to be spread as usual and the Roller run over
the patches.
(7). That in each district the most expensive roads, with the
Ilighest price per perch per annum, should be first dealt with.
(8). One hundred and one miles of intpcrtant roads leadinv
out of the towns of Ardee, Drogll€da and Dundalk are now
maintained at an expense of per annum, and there is no
room for doubt that at least one-fourth of this amount, CI,IIG
per annum, is wasted. The quantity of broken stones supplied
and spmead in each year on these 101 miles of road amounts to,
16,600 cubic yards, and at least one-fourth of these stones are
ground into dust, to be afterwards swept off as mud, before
the patches of stones are rolled smooth by the traffic. A Roller
roils the, broken stones to a smooth and hard surface withaut
any waste. The existing wastage of El, 116 per annum would
be about sufficient to keep three Rollers at work. One Roller
only roll a boul twelve miles per annunl, dealing in a proper
Six years ago I wrot.e to the other County Surveyors, and
found that thirteen counties had adopted Steam Ikhlling. Since
then several other counties have followed suit, including Mon-
In 1903 Co Tyrone bought one Roller and
aghan and Meath.
cautiously watched for results. Now Tyrone has twelve Rollers
at work, ancl BVO more Rollers have been ordered. The valua-
fion of Tyrone is nearly, and the valuation of Louth
is In this proportion, Tyrone having fourteen
Rollers, Louth ought to have at least seven WI lers. It is a
re;narkable fact that in this respect Louth ran ks with the
most backward counties ir all Ireland. There is not one single,
Road Roller employ€TI in the Louth Rural Districts.

Letter and Order from the Department, dated the 21st ult.y
as under: —
No. 2665/10
Department of Agriculture,
And Technical Instruction for Ireland,
(Veterinary Branch),),
13, Upper Merrion St.,
21st June, 1910.
Animals (Notification of Disease) (Ireland) Order
of 1910.
Sir,
The Department of Agriculture and Technical In-
strnction for Ireland desire LO forward herewith, for the infor-
6
nation of your Local Authority, some copies of the above-
named Order, which has been made by tne Department, re-
quiring the notification by Veterinary Surgeons or Veterinary
Practitioners of any. cases of contagious or infectious disease
of certain specified kinds which may come before them in pri-
vate practice, and providing, among other thngs, for the pay-
ment by Local Authorities of a fee of two shillings and six-
pence in respect of each such notification.
This Order, which comes into force on 1st proximo, will give
•effect in Ireland to the Aöt passed on the subject last year,
entitled the "Diseases of Animals Act, 1909," a copy of which
will also he found enclosed.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
DISEASES OF ANIMALS ACT, 1909.
Chapter 26.
An Act i o provide for the Payment of Fees to Veterinary
Surgeons and Practitioners for Notification of Diseases of
Animals.
(25th November, 1909).
Be it enacted by the King's Most Excellent Majesty, by and
•with the advice and consent of the Lords Spiritual and Tem-
poral, and Commons, in this present Parliament assem bled,
.and by the authority ot the same as follows :
2. This Aoet may be cited as the Diseases of Animals Act,
Animals Acts, 1894 to 1903. shall pay to a veterinary surgeon
or veterinary practitioner, in respect of every notification of
•disease made by him to the local authority or any officer of the
local authority in pursuance of an order under those Acts re-
quiring such a notification, such fee not exceeding two shil-
l.•ngs and sixpence as may be prescribed by the order.
7
2. This Act may be cited as the Diseases of Animals Acts,
and shall be construed as one with the Diseases of Animals
Acts, 1894 to 1903, and those Acts and this Act may be cited
together the Diseases of Animals Acts, 1894 to 1909.

Letter from the Department, dated 25th inst, No. 3642/10>
as under : —
IMPORTATION OF ANIMALS (IRELAND) ONDER OF 19T
FOOT ANI) MOUTH DISEASE.
Memorandum.
The Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction
for Ireland desire to intimate to all whom it may concern,
that owing to the reported occurrence of an outbreak of Foot
and Mouth Disease in Great Britain, all importation of Cattle,
Sheep, Goats, or other ruminating animals, and Swine, into
Ireland from that country are, for the time being, prohibited.
It is to be understood that equine animals do not eome•
within Che scope of this prohibition, and that, consequently,
Horses, Asses, and Mules may still be imported from across
the Chan 1€•1, if accompanied on each occasion by the necessary
permit from the Department.

IRISH BREWERS' ASSOCIATION.
Resolved :
That inasmuch as many of the smaller Irish
eries in face of mammoth competition have to struggle to con«
tinue their business, the increased taxation of last year sought
to be re:imposed by the Budget introduced by the Chancellor
of the Exchequer for 1910-11 is a real hardship, and will un-
doubtedly be the means of breaking up some of these firms,.
and still further reducing the number of small Breweries in
Ireland which give so much employment, encourage tillage,
und are a distinct benefit to the district in which they are
situated in many ways, as for instance, in providing food-stuffs
such as grain and malt oombings for cattle feeding, and in
keeping alive a healthy competition in the purchase of Barley,.
Fodder, ete
The increased Whiskey Tax has, unfortunately, seriously
affected the Irish Distilleries, causing thereby a distinct loss
to Farmers and Agriculture generally, as well as loss of employ-
ment, and if, in addition to this, Preweries are closed down,
the future prospects of tillage in Ireland must be seriously
jured.
In face of these circumstances, this Council urgently re-.
quests the Irish members of Parliament to insist that they
smaller Trish Breweries be relieved of the extra and excessive.
burdens itnposed lay last year's Budget.
Proposed by Mr James Wynne,
Seconded by Mr Bernard Hamill, J P,
That the foregoing resolution from the Irish Brewers' Assow
ciation be and is hereby adopted by this Council.
Amendment moved by Mr Joseph T Dolan, seconded by Mr
C A Duffy, J P—That the resolution be marked "read."
On a division there voted for the amendment :—Messrs Dolan,
T J; Donegan, Duffy, C A; Finnegan, King, M 'Gee, O'Meara..
Woods, P; and Wocds, Wm—Io.
10
Against the amendment there voted :—Messrs Byrne, T J ,
Dolan, Joseph; Hamill, B; Macardle, T C; M'Court and
W ynne—6.
The Chairman, therefore, declared the amendment carried by
IO votes to 6.
The following discussion took place respecting the introduc-
tion of Electric Light to the County Courthouse:—
LIGHT.
Mr sl Comerford, Town Clerk, Dundalk, stated that he had
been deputed by the Urban Council to wa.u on them for the
purpose of getting an undertaking that they would take
•electric light into the building if it were as cheap as gas. He
•explained that the Urban Council were promoting an Electric
Lighting scheme, and that he required the signature of the
•Chairman to such an undertaking, the entire list to be sent
to the L G B.
Mr Hamill said that when the County Surveyor was about
making application for new gas fittings in the building he
asked him to postpone it, as they were promoting an Electric
Lighting scheme.
Mr King—I don't object to it, but I would like to know
if we adopt it would there be any extra cost with regard to
putting In new fittings?
Mr MaeardIe—Certainly. 'file fittings are all rotten as they
are, and you want new ones (laughter).
Mr M 'Court said that if they continued witn gas they would
have to undergo an expenditure of at least C4() owing to the
fittings. When the question came forward they could get an
.estimate oc what electrical fittings would cost.
Mr Duffy—Mr Lynam estimated that the gas fittings would
cost COO, and we postponed it on account Of this F, lectric
Lighting scheme.
The necessary undertaking was given, and Mr Comerford
thanked the Council..

MARGARINE BILL.
under-mentioned communication from Mr Denis Kil-
M P, was read and approved of by the Council
House of Commons,
z
June 20th, 1910.
Dear Sir,
I am again re-introducing the "Margarine Bill," which nas
for its object the prevention of Margarine being coloured as a
•colourable imitation of butter.
Yon will remember that the Bill was defeated in Committee
last Session after passing Its second reading without a division.
This was uue to the opposition of the Grocers' Association and
the Margarine manufacturers combined, who do not relish the
idea of se!ling margarine in its original colour, which is white,
thus end once and for all the possibility of palming it off
as butter.
It may be pointed out that the English farmer is not hit
by the substitution of margarine for butter, his great aim
being the more profitable one of supplying the big cities with
milk, which is being demanded in ever increasing quantities.
In the first five months of this year the increase in the
import of margarine has been over tons, and this does
not take into account the vast quantities being manufactured
in the United Kingdom from oil and animal fat. Ireland alone
stands to lose, and if the present state of things is allowed to
•continue Irish butter must go to the wall.
Last SesÄ1011 1 asked a question with regard to some samples
'Of ' 'Irish Butter" purchased by the Inspectors of the Depart-
ment of Agriculture for Ireland, both in London and Liver-
pool, and what was the reply—that "out of JIG samples pur-
chased as 'Irish Butter' only 16 were genuine," and this, be
it remenn»ered, in the two greatest cities in the Empire.
During the last few years thousands of acres of land have
been divided among occupiers, uneconomic holding have been
•enlarged, while under the operations of the newly formed
€congested Districts Board—particularly in the counties west
12
of the Shannon—vast tracts of land hitherto ' 'held up" wilh
now for the first time come under cultivation.
Instead of the "rigid and rotten communities" you will soon.
have hundreds of small well cultivated farms, ideal land for
tillage so necessary to Winter dairying, where formerly you
had only the shC1)herd and his dog.
Under snell conditions you are bound to have a vast increase.
in the production og butter ia the near future: and the question
naturally forces itself—can a market be found for it under fair
conditions ? Will fraudulent substitution be allowed to con-
tinue?
If the Bill now introduced for the second time can
be passed into law the future of Irish butter is safe, and I
trust I may have the support of your Council, and that you:
will strengthen my hands by passing a resolution in its favour-
and sending it to the Press.
I am, etc,
D. KILBRIDE.

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
SUBK'OMMITTEE OX STEAM ROLLING, 27th JUNE, 191(h
Mr Chairman,
I beg to That a Steam Roller and Plant be
purchased by the County Council at a price not exceeding.
$680, and that C32() be provided for repmrs dulling a period'
Of ten years, provided that the Rural District Councils adopt
Steam Rolling.
• (2). That if the Rural Councils adopt Steam li'olling„
for the above purpose be borrowed from the Commis-
sioners of Public Works at 31 per cent—principal and interest
to be paid off by ten annual payments of Cll(j 12s 7d each. the
payments to be levied off the County-atluarge, Urban districts.
excepted.
(3). That the R ural Councils be asked to formulate pro-
posals for the working expenses of a Roller. The working
expenses include the wages of the engine driver, flagman and
foreman spreader, coal and coke, oil, etc, and are estimated
at 22s per day for 240 working days, or C2(j4 per annum. A
Roller could be kept at work if Ardee No. I Rural District
would• pass C99 for 90 working clays, Drogheda (Louth) C,32•
IGs for 48 days, and Dundalk El 12 4s for 102 days—total,
C2ß4 for 240 days' rolling. Unless the Rural District Councils
agree to pay for working expenses, in proportion to the num ber
of days rolling done in each district, rolling cannot be carried
on, and the matter must be dropped.
(4). That, if Rolling be adopted by the Rural Councils,
a Steam Rolling Account bo opened to show the total working•
expenses of the Roller for each year (including half wages for
the threo men during stoppages, clue to aceidents, repairs and'
frost), also the number of days' rolling done in each district,
and the amount in proportion charged to each district. This
account to be open to inspection 'by ratepayer in the Rural.
Districts on payment of a small fee.
(5). That, if Rolling be adopted and existing contracts ex-
Pire, the County Surveyor be directed to inelude in each new
contract on principa! roads a specified number of days' rolling
—about one day's rolling to each 40 or 50 cubie yards of broken
16
.stones.
The Roller to. be supplied to contractors free of
charge, but the contractor to be bound to have sufficient men
to spread the broken stones before the Roller quickly, provide
six men to blind and sweep, and a driver and horse for one
water cart, or, if necessary, for two water carts. Also the
.contrac€or should cart coal for the engine—about 4 cwts per
day—and a contractor should be liable to penalties if he
delays the Roller and keeps it idle.
(C). That contractors not now bound to employ a Roller be
asked to volunteer to use it' under the conditions as above
(paragraph 5). About one-third of the length of a contract
to be heavily sheeted with broken stones and rolled in each
year, the remaining two-thirds to have ruts darned. If a
-sufficient number of contractors do not volunteer, then the
purchase cf a Roller to be postponed for a year or two, or the
broken stones to be spread as usual and the Roller run over
•the patches.
(7). That in each district the most expensive roads, with
the highest price per perch per annum, should be first dealt
•with.
(8). One hundred and one miles of important roads leading
of Lhe towns of Ardee, Drogheda and Dundalk are now
maintained at an expense of C4.4G4 per annum, and there is
no room for doubt that at least one-fourth of this amount,
El ,116 per annum, -is wasted. The quantity of broken stoness
supplied { nd in each year on these 101 miles of road
amounts 16, GOO cubie yards, and at least one-fourth of these
stones are ground into dust, to be afterwards swept off as mud,
'before the patches of stones are rolled smooth by the traffic.
A Roller rolls the broken stones to a smooth and hard surface
without any waste. The existing wastage of ELI 16 per annum
would be about sufficient to keep three Rollers at work. One
Roller can only roll about twelve miles per annum, dealing in
a proper manner with 36 mules in three years.
Six years ago r wrote to the other County Surveyors, and
found that thirteen counties had adopted Steam Rolling.
Since then several other counties have followed suit, including
Monaghan and Meath. In 1903 Co Tyrone bought one l?oller
and cautiously watched for results. Now Tyrone has twelve
Rollers at work and two more Rollers have been ordered. The
17
value lion of Tyrone is nearly, and the valuation of
Louth is In this proportion, Tyrone having four-
teen Rollers, Louth ought to have at least seven Rollers. It
is a remarkable fact that in this respect Louth ranks with
the most backward counties in all Ireland. There is not one
single Road Roller employed in the Louth Rural Districts.
I am, Gentlemen, your obedient Servant,
P. J. LYNAM,
County Surveyor of Louth.

Sir,

acknowledge the receipt of your let-ter of the 21st instant, and,
in reply, to inform .Nou that the Board do make loans for the
purchase of Steam Road Rollers and the necessary appurten-
anceS thereto. If it is desired to obtain a loan from public
funds appiication should, in the first instance, be made to the
Local Government Board, whose recommendation of a loan will
be necessary before this Board can entertain the matter. The
rates of interest at present a.ppuieable to loans charged on the
security of local rates are as follows per cent when the
period does not exceed 30 years ; 3} per cent when the period
does not exceed 50 years.

ARMAGH LETTER.
Dear Sir,
in the
Dublin
I am in receipt of your letter of the 15th inst making in-
regarding the purchase and use of Steam Road Rollers
in tho county. In answer to your queries I beg to state that
(1). Steam Rolling was adopted here by the County Council
on its own initiative.
Ihave no recollection of the District
Councils being consulted.
(2). I dc not think the li'nral District Councils were par-
ticularly favourable, hut I do not remember any formal
objections.
(3). The cost of the Rollers and the larger road machinery
has always been a County (Rural) charge.
Tt is many years since this Council began purchasing Steam
Rolle•s, but the above answers are, to the best of my belief,
correct.
Yours faithfully,
F. A. REID,
Assistant Secretary.
COUNTY OF DOWN.
Dear Sir,
In reply to yours of 15th instant with reference to the sub-
jeet of Steam Rolling, the Rural District Councils in the first
instance form Illate proposals, which are sent forward to the
County Council for consideration. The Rural Dastrict Councils
were consulted before the adoption of Steam Rolling in this
county.
When Steam Rolling was first adopted here, all the Rural
Councils adopted it, with the exception of one. In the latter
case the County Council took no action. However, Steam
Rt.)lling is now carried out in all the Rural Districts.
21
The cost of Steam Rolling is levied according to the charge-
ability of the road on which the work .is performed, i.e., on
a main road it is a half county and half district charge, and
on a district road, wholly a district charge.
Yours faithfully,
J. L. YOUNG,
Deputy Sec. to the County CounciL
COUNTY OF DUBLIN.
Dear Sir,
Your letter of the 15th inst to the Secretary of this Council
has been referred to me. In reply I have to state—
(I). It was not at the request of the Rural District Councils.
Steam Rolling wag adopted, but they were consulted before
its adoption.
(2). One of the five Rural District Councils, after having
some years ago adopted Steam Rolling for one year, rejected
it for some years until the present year, when they have agam
adopted it, so that at present all five Itural Districts have
adopted Steam Rolling.
(3). The purchase of the Rollers and rolling plant, and the
larger repairs of same only, are made a County at Large
(ex-Urbnn) charge. The cost of the working and maintenance
of the Rollers is charged to the district in which the work is
being done.
Yours faithfully,
W. COLLEN,
County Surveyor.
MONAGHAN COUNTY COUNCIL.
Sir,
In reply to yours of the 15th ultiuo, the Road Machinery was
obtained for this county by the County Council to improve the
roads. The Rural Districts were asked for their support to
the scheme. Some objected, others did not. A proposal is
now submitted by the County Surveyor to each District.
22
Counc:l ot its quarterly meeting for a certain sum for Steam
rolling.
The expenses of the scheme are a County-at-Large (Rural)
charge, and is charged on all the Rural Districts.
Yours truly,
DENIS CAROLAN RUSHE,
Secretary.

27
The undermentioned letter from the L. G. Board was read,
•and a copy was ordered to be sent to each Collector :—
No. 64,127/1910. Louth County.
Local Government Board, Dublin,
14th October, 1910.
Sir,
I am directed by the Lecal Government Board for
Ireland to inform you that they have had before them your
letter of the 3rd instant, with enclosure, relative to the collec-
tio.a of the poor rate for the past half-year in the County of
Louth ; and, with reference thereto, I am to state that tho
Board much regret to observe that the collection is in an ex-
tremely unsatisfactory condition, there being a sum of no less
than Lf6,3ö7 out of a total of C14,893 outstanding at the close
of the half-year.
This discreditable state of affairs must be entirely attributed
to the failure of the Collectors to proceed with their collections
in the earlier poi fion of the half-year. The Board would point
•out that, although the Collectors received tneir warranta so
far •back as the 3rd of June last, they had, notwithstanding
the repeated warnings given to them, only lodged a sum of
C453 by the 22nd July, while by the 10th of September, or
three weeks before the close of the half-year, the large sum of
CII,G09 remained uncollected.
The Board further observe that the large proportion of the
•rates which the Collectors have lodged was collected during
the month of September, at a time when the Collectors state
that they were continuously employed at the Revision Courts,
and that it was åbsolutely impossible for them to attend to the
•duties of their collections. It is therefore quite evident that
the Collectors must have made little or no effort to prosecute
their collections during the months of June, July, and Au-
gust.
As regards the request of the Finance Committee that ex-
•eeptional treatment should be afforded to County Louth, I am
to state that the Board have nothing to add to their letter of
*the 18th Novembev last, in reply to a sitnilar request then
28
In other agricultural districts:
made by the County Council.
which are much less favourably circumstanced than County-
Louth, and where the difficulties of collection are greater, thev
Collectors have again this year been able to lodge the full'
amount of their warrants within the prescribed time, and tho
Board see no ground for making an exception in the case of •
County Louth to the rule that prevails elsewhere throughout
Ireland as to the date of closing the rates.
I am to add that the law requires the Collectors to collect
and lodge each moiety of tho rate within the half-year, and if
the Collectors in County Louth are not prepared to carry out
the law in this respect, they should be called on to tender
their resignation, so that proper arrangements may be made-
for the collection of the poor rate in the county.
The County Council should require the Collectors to lodge•
the outstanding amounts of the first moiety without any fur—
ther delay.
T am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS,
Secretary.
To the Secretary to the
Louth County Council.
The following communication from the Chief
Office, was also read by the Secretary :—
Chief Secretary's Office,
Dublin Castle,
Secretary's
12th October, 1910.
Sir,
I am directed by the Lord Lieutenant to transmit
to you herewith an Order for payment of the sum of C(i38 2s Sd
in respect of the cost of maintenance of pauper lunatics for
29
the year ended 31st March, 1910, in the Richmond District
Lunatic Asylum.
I am to state that owing to the insufficiency of the amount
paid under Section 58 of the Local Government (Ireland) Act,
1898, to the Local Taxation (Ireland) Account in the current
financial year, to meet the sums payable thereout in the same
period under the Section ,as amended by the Local Govern-
ment (Ireland) Act, 1902, His Excellency has deemed it neces-
'sary, in pursuance of the provisions of Sub-section (5) of SC c-
tion 58, to direct a proportionate abatement of the sums so
. payable havi:rg regard to the said insufficiency.
The money available in the Account at the present date is
insufficient to pay a larger ptx»portion than 68 per cent. of the
•estimated charges falling to oe met from the Account, and
the amount included in the accompanying Order represents
the proportion, calculated on this basis, of the amount certified
by the Auditor to be payable to the Asylum, less the sum of
Cl,700 already advanced.
I am to add that with the payment into the Account early
in 1911 of the sum of mentioned in Sub-Section (1) (b)
of Section 58, His Excellency expects to be rin a position to
make a further payment from the Account to local authorities,
but the measure of the additional relief that may be thus
afforded cannot be definitely stated until the precise amount of
all the charges falling to be met under the Act oi 1898 and the
Amending Act of 1902 shall have been ascertained towards tho
•close of the present financial year.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
J. J. CULLINAN.
The Secretary,
County Council of Louth.

SPECIFICATION OF WORKS.
The works sha.! eon.sist of the excavation of a channel aercss
strand, a swinging basin sheltered by a breakwater, and Of
a quay or quays, all in accordance with one or other of the
alternative phvv prepared by the Engineer to the Department
of Agriculture conferenee, and accompanying this specifica-
tion or such modified plans as may be adopted.
The melhod oi the excavation of the works shall ans ease
be as follows
The channel shall be dredged to a depth of 13ft Gin below
the coping Hoes's quay, this being the depth estimated
as necessary give 11 ft Gin of water in the channel at high
water of neap tides, and shall have a width of not less than
at bottom. with slopes not steeper than 4 to 1.
The basin sl,ai.i be excavated to the area shown OA •one
other of the plans, and the slopes there indicated.
The breakwater shall be eonstruetecl of eoncrete to the spe-
eification giv •below, and to be of the shown, its
toe being protected by an apron where i adicated. Its extrem-
i!ty shall, further be protected by sheet piling if necessary.
A portion of the spoil from the excavations shall be deposited
at the back of the breakwater, so as •to form a beach there to
protect it, as indicated on the general plan.
The quays Shai! be constructed eonerete walls, with filling
of sand O/ Shiiygle,' the Walls ,lieiåg of section shown.- ' if

That this Council hereby consent to an Order being made by

Plants of the following species, viz—Thistles, Docking, Rag-
worth and Coltsfoot, are noxious weeds for Ohe purposes of the
Weeds and Agricultural Seeds (Ireland) A ot, 1909.
4
'The Chairman divided the Council on this matter, with the
following result
FOI +,he Resolution—Messrs T J Byrne, Patrick Byrne,.

The following correspondence from the Department respect-
ing the Regulations as to the wearing of Collars by Dogs
No. I—No dog shall be allowed to in any highway or
place of public resort in the Administrative County of Louth
unless such dog is wearing, while in such hiuhway or place of
resort, a collar with the name and address of the Owner of
such dog inscribed thereon, or a plate or badge attached
thereto.
5
EXEMPTION FROM REGULATIONS
The foregoing Regulation does not apply to any pack of
hounds, OF to any dog while being used for sporting purposes,
or for the capture or destruction of vermin, or for the driving
or tending of cattle or sheep.
SEIZURE OF DOGS WHERE REGULATIONS ARE
CONTRAVENED.
Any dog in respect of which an offence is being committed
may be seized and treated as a stray dog under the powers
•conferred by Section 3 of the Dogs Act, 1906.
OFFENCES.
If any dog found in a highway or place of public resort is
not wearing a collar as prescribed by th•e Regulations, the
Owner of t;he dog, and the person for the time 'being ill charge
thereof, and the person allowing the same to be in the\highway,
or in the -place of public resort, in contravention of such
Regulations, shall, each according to and in respect of his own
acts and defautts, be deemed guilty of an offence against the
Diseases of Animals Act, 1894.

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
The following report from the County Surveyor was read :—
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
1. At Oriel Demesne, and at Rokeby Hall, weak 3rd class
roads have been damaged by heavy timber traffic, and sums
of C'20 and C25 have been passed to supply extra broken stones
in addition to the specified quantity (se No 2, Ardee, and No
3, Drogheda). No. 1, Drogheda is a usefu; Lane, now to be
repaired as a 4th class road.
2. Ardee and Dundalk Rural Districts have refused the
Free Grants offered by the •Road Board, applications for
equivalent sums, to be raised by the Poor Rate, being rejected.
I cannot help thinking that this decision was arrived at on
ingufficient information. Members of the Councils seem not
to have read the report of your deputation which attended
the Road Board Conference on 7th Marcrn last, or the verbatim
report of the Chairman's speech, which was published in the
Dublin papers on the 10th March. The Chairman of the
Road Board, Sir George Gibb, stated that before two years a
Bill would be promoted in Parliament tc levy ld in the C off
every county in Ireland, the money to be handed over to the
Road Board, to be expended by them on about 2,000 miles
of the principal roads in Ireland. This proposal is so objection-
able and so universally disapproved that it will probably be
dropped, if it has not been dropped already. Mr Nolan, M P,
informed the Ardee Council that he could hear nothing of
such a Bill, and that if introduced it would certainly be
rej ected.
3. This year and next year, élowever, there is a totally
(Efferent state of things. In each year a free grant of CI,200
is offered to County Louth, provided that an equal amount be
raised by the District Councils, to be spent on improvements,
steam rolling and improved road metal, on 36 miles of the most
important and most: expensive roads in the county. This
money could be expended to such advantage that it seems to
me to be unwise to refuse it.
4. Good road metal is the first essential for good and cheap
roads. Soft stones make bad and expensive roads, and the
question of good quarries is most important. Braganstown
quarry supplies over twenty miles of roads round Castlebelling-
ham, and six road contractors are now at work in this quarry
under, per9haps, the worst possible conditions. The clay and
the soft top of the rock are flung anywhere. quarry is not
drained, and no one contractor will do any pumping, conse-
quntly the quarry is so shallow that the clay and rubbish dealt
with are nearly equal in bulk to the stones quarried. When
really good stone is met it is passed by as too hard to quarry
and break. The approach road to the quarry is a clay track,
impassible in bad weather. Dunleer quasrry is in much the
same state. If these or similar quarries wero to 'be purehased
and properly workal by direeb labour, the sale of stones to
road eon tractors at a fixed wice would pay the working
expenses when the first cost was paid off, and the free grants
from the Road Board Offer an opportuuitv which it would be
a pity to miss. Direct labour scattered over hundreds of miles
of roads cannot be properly supervised, but there is a very
different state of things in a quarry which supplies a measured
quantity of broken stones every year. Better stones in future
would make better roads and also cheaper roads, because the
quantity of broken stones to be supplied might be reduced iny
as much as 40 per cent, and there would not be as nytvch mud bo
8
sweep off. At Beliurgan, Ballymakellett, etc, there is a
practically unlimited supply of excellent stone, perhaps the
best in the British Isles, which ought to be much more widely
used. English Councils find that it pays them to Ahi,p similar
stones from Carlingf01<l.
I am, Gentlemen, your cybetlient servant,
P. J. LYN AM, County Surveyor.
Proposed by Mr Edwa1Tl Goodman,
Seconded by Mr Donegan, J P, and
Resolved-—Thnt the Report of the County Surveyor as ap-
poaring on page, 1 on Schedule of Proposuls and is hereby
adopted by this Council ; that application be made to the Road
Boat•d for corresponding amounts passzd by the Drogheda and
Dundalk Ul ban Districts aryl Drogheda (Louth) Rural District
as soon as specifications are ledged.

Union Workhouse, Dundalk,

Dear Sir,

as uniform time has now 'been ad:jpted by
France and other countries 111 Western Europe, largely
the interest of t;heir export trade, and as, combined by the
disadvantages of being some, 25 minutes behind Greenwich
tinie, we have an increasingly slow telegraplne service with
great trade centres in England and Scotland, the time lifts
come, in the opinion of this Council, when, in the interest of
the export and general trade of the country, Greenwich time
should be adopted in Ireland, and we wish to convey to other
Public Bodies and Chambers of Commerce who have taken
this and the question of telegraphic service with the Govern-
ment, that they can count on our most earnest co-operation.
The Council ordered that this matter appear on the next
agenda issued by the Secretary.

LOUTH COUNTS COUNCIL SCHOLARSHIPS
TO THE NATIONAL UNIVERSITY.
The County Council of Louth offer Two Scholarships of Cö()
each, providing a Year's Course at Dublin University College.
The ,SGholarships will be awarded to the two applicants who
show highest merit in the Test Examination, tund who Matric-
ulate in September, 1911, or who have already Matriculated
•but have not gone through the first University Year's Course,
and who fulfil the other conditions, viz: -
(1) A Good Stardard of HonourS in the Examination.
(2) A Knowledge of irisih.
(3) Eligibility in Health, Character and Ago.
(4) Three Years' Residence of Parents or Guardians in
County Louth.
(5) Want, of sufficient means to follow a University Course
at their own expense.

Tho Intermediate Examination Results Of 1911 and 1910 will
be used as a Competitive Test, but Candidates may be re-
•quired to submit to another Examination, of which due notice
Will 'be given them; and if any should not have taken Irish • n
the Intermediate they must undergo Special Examination
in Irish, to be arranged by the County Council.
Applications must be sent to the Secretary of the County
Council, Dundalk, on or before September 15th.

Laurence Brannigan, Drogheda.

chin's, Limerick :—
".At the request of Mr Dolan, I beg to send you the award
1
would make •between the two Scholarship candidates. I shall
call the candidates boy and girl. Taking printed marks the boy
gets 347, the girl 508. Comparing thegyourses, the boy rend
the honours course in three subjects ; ; the girl in five. tho
boy passed with honours only in Irish, thc girl in English
Domestic Economy and Hygiene. In languages •the boy got
honours in Irisih, passed in the honours course in French, and
reached mere pass level in Latin and English. The girl got
honours in English, passed in honours course in Latin and
French, and reached mere pass level in Irish. In mathematics
the boy did not take the honours course in any subject, and
pasgéd in, two out of three. The girl took t'.te honours
in two subjects, and passed in that course one of theny

boy failed by a few marks to get honours: the girl got ner

the balance of advantage in her favour is very considerable. '
After very considerable discussion on, this matter, the Counoil

and that the other Scholarship be divided between Miss Mae-.
Parland and Laurence Brannigan.

was eventually
Proposed by Mr Denis J Rath,

21
Amendment moved by Mr Patrick O'Connell,

That we hereby agree to guarantee a sum of not more t,ha•)
towards Che expenses which may be incurred in obtaining
a Provisional Order for the proposed Pier at Annagassan,
provided the promoters submit a draft copy of the Provision 11
Order for the consideration of t/he Council for their approval
or rejection, and that this. resolution be stibmitted to -'he
Department for their consideration.
On a division there voted for the amendment—Messrs Byrne,
T J ; Connolly, James; Kearney, Joseph P; Murphy, Jolm ;
M 'Court, James; O'Connell, Patrick ; and Skeffington, Wm
Against the amendment there voted—Messrs Byrne, Patrick ;
Dolan, Joseph; Hamill, Bernard; Rath, Denis J; and Woods,
Peter—5.
The Chairman therefore declared the amendment carried
by 7 votes to 5.

MOTOR CAR LICENCE DUTIES.
FINANCE (1909-10) ACT, 1910.
Loea:l Government Board.
Dublin, '29th November, 1911.
I am directed by tlhe Local Government for
land to state that, in connection with the step : to taken for
tho eolleetion of the duties of licences motar cars under the
Provisions of t,he Motor Car Licence, Duties (Ireland) Col loe-
tinn Order, 1910, the Ccuncils of Conn ties and County Bor-
12
onghs have, several instances, applied to the Board for ad-
vice on questions which have arisen in the levying of the clu-
ties.
The Board i ave thought it desirable to prepare a memoran-
dum for the guidance of local authorities in Ireland as to their
powers and dl'ties witlil respect to the collection of Motor Car
Licence Duties and they accordingly direct me to forward to
you six comes o: the memorandum which contains a summary of
tho statutory provisions and the procedure In relation to these
duties.
The menu rm dum has been drawn up in consultation with
• His Majesty •g Treasury, the Postmaster-General and the Boal'(l
of Customs a•y,•. Excuse. and it is hoped that the information
afforded, while not exfnau.stive, will cover most of the points
with regard to which difficulties appear to have arisen up to
the present.
The memorandum will be placed on sale and copies may be
shortlv obtained. either directly or through any booksel lev,
from Mr E Person,by, Crafton Street, Dublin.
I am, Sir,
Your Obedient Servant,
A. R. BARI.AS,
Secreta ry.
The Secretary of each County Council.
The Town •Clerk of each Chunty Borough.

mended—That the Secretary be appointed to carry out, the
duties nuder the Motor Car Act, 1909-10, and that he reeeive
the sum of C12 this year (31st Marell, 1911) and that •in future
the 5 per eenc or. value of Licenses issued during , tho year, as
allowed by Treasury: be now allowed to our Secretary. Tlre

APPOINTMENT OF TWO MEMBERS ON THE
COUNCIL OF AGRICULTURE.
In connection With the foregoing the Secretary read
the following letter :
Departnoent of Agriculture and
Technical Instruction for Irelan(l,
Upper Merrion Street, Dublin,
11th December, 1911.
COUNCIL OF AGRICULTURE.
Sir—I have to acquaint you, for the information of
your Council,
that the term of office of the present
Council of Agriculture will expire on the 31st March,
1912, and to enclose a copy of the regulations for the
appointment of members of the new Council.
The regulations immediately concerning your Council
are Nos. 2, 5, 6, 8, and 11, which provide f0T the ap-
pointment• of two persons, who need not necessarily be
members of your Council, to serve on the Council of
Agriculture in accordance with Section 8 of the Agricul-
ture and Technical Instruction (Ireland) Act, 1899.
elt will be observed that under the regulations now
Issued the appointment of representatives by the County
Council may take place at any meeting of the County
6
Council held between ISt January and ISt April, 1912.
This arrangement has been made in order to obviate the
inconvenience of a special meeting of the County Coun-
cil for the purpose of the election. The new represen-
tatives will, of course, not assume office until ISt April,
1912.
By Section 27 (1) of the Act of 1899, the mernbers of
the Council of Agriculture hold office for terms oi three
years, and may be re-appointed.
A supply of copies of the regulations is sent under
separate cover to enable you to comply with the pro-
visions of Regulation No. 11. Further copies may be
had on application.
I am to request that, in consultation with the Chair-
man, you will have the necessary formal notice given on
the Agenda paper for the meeting at which the appoint-
ments in question will be made; and I am also to ask
you to inform the Department in due course of the date
of that meeting.
I am, sir,
Your obedient servant,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.
The Secretary, Louth County Council.
Proposed by Mr. Thomas Maguire,
Seconded by Mr. Edward Goodman, and

Section 24 of the Agriculture and Technical Instruction
(Ireland) Act, 1899, viz. :
Mr. Jas. NIcCarthy, J.P., Newfoundwell, Drogheda ; and

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
The following Report from the County Surveyor Was
read :
Mr. Chairman and Gentlemen;
Your County Surveyor has made the usual appli-
cations to your Rural Councils for the renewal of ex-
piring maintenance contracts, which have been ap-
proved by them.
Nearly all of these have been tendered for, and will
corne before you on Form 20.
Special works have been passed in Drogheda Rural
to the amount of C 460, and special works in Dundalk,
L 158 IS. od.
Some small damages have occurred along your coast
during the high tides this month, two of which are
under repair on Sudden Damage Orders. The other,
at Blackrock, can, I consider, wait for ordinary pro-
posal, at next District Council Meeting.
During the last year nine contractors, as follow, have
failed to carry out their contracts, which have been
taken up by the County Surveyor in accordance with
9
Clause No. 21 of the Specification. I beg to recomnoend
that these contracts be cancelled, and new contracts are
_now applied for

The following correspondence was read
Francis Street, Dundalk,
24th February, 1912.
Re TUL7.v NEW ROAD.
Dear Sir,
With reference .to letter of 18th ult. from the
Dundalk Rural District Council, which I return herewith,
to the effect that a resolution was unanimously passed
recommending the County Council to release Patrick
Connolly from his contract for the making of above road,
I desire to state that I can find no express power of
release given to the County Council in the contract and
I am of opinion that the
bond of the 24th April, 1911.
County Council can compel the contractor to proceed
with the work, subject to the grant of easement being
14
executed by the Rural District Council, which has not
yet been done.- The contract gives the County Surveyor
authority to grant an extension of time for completion to
the contractor in such circumstances.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
T. F. Filgate, Esq., sec.
Louth County Council, Dundalk.
Referred back to District Council.
Dundalk Rural District,
18th January, 1912.
Dear Sir,
At their Quarterly Meeting, held on the 8th
instant, the Council of this District unanimously passed
a resolution recommending the County Council to release
Patrick Connolly of his contract for the making of a new
road at Tully, in consequence of the long delay in per-
mitting him to commence the work.
The District Council apply for permission to open
portion of the road at Lordship, for the purpose of pro-
viding an improved water supply there.
Yours faithfully,
P. R. FINIGAN,
Clerk of the Council.
To T. F. Filgate, Esq.
Francis Street, Dundalk,
24th February, 1912.
Re CLERK OF THE PEACE'S REGISTRATION EXPENSES.
Dear Sir,
With reference to the application of the Clerk
of the Peace in above, dated the 25th October, 1911, for
the sum of C 140 3s. 9d, payment of which amount I am
informed has been made, it is quite clear that he has re-
ceived remuneration for what is part of his duty by
charging the full scale irrespective of actual expenditure•
I have read the Privy Council Order of the 21st Nov-
ember, 1898, rescinding the previous Order of the 3rd
June, 1889, and on its construction there can be no doubt
that only the actual expenses of the Clerk of the Peace
when duly vouched, but necessarily within the scale,
may be paid. The scale is set out in the Order, and same
"shall be, and the same is hereby fixed, as the scalewithin
which the actual expenses, when duly vouched, exclusive
Of the costs of necessary printing, to be incurred by and
paid to a Clerk of the Peace for carrying into effect the
provisions of the Acts relating to the Registration of
Voters, or otherwise in respect of such Acts, shall be
calculated."
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
T. F. Filgate, Esq„ Sec.
Louth County Council, Dundalk.
Clerk's ()ffice, Union Workhouse,
Ardee, 27th day of February, 1912.
Dear Sir,
The Ardee No. 1 Rural District Council have
to-day had under their consideration the question of the
proposed increase of fourpence in the in the General
Rate on this District for the ensuing year, and they
desire to bring under the notice of the Finance Com-
mittee of the Louth County Council the following cir-
cumstances which have arisen since the Ardee estimates
were sent in, showing, as they believe, how a reduction
in the County estimate can be carried out justifiably,
and the District Council would recommend to the Com-
mittee the expediency of giving effect to the reductions
indicated—
new Dietary Scale has, on the recommendation
Of the Local Government Board Inspector, been intro-
duced into the Workhouse, and a couple of weeks' trial
Of it has resulted in a saving of LI 14s 6d per week, and
as there is no reason to think that this saving cannot be
maintained, it is calculated that the gain for the year
will be 190.
2—1t is expected that a number of the inmates of the
16
\'Vorkhouse will this year qualify for the Old Age Pension,
with a possible saving to the Union of L 80.
3—The Rural District Council's estimate includes , a
year's repayment of a new loan originally amounting, to
C 1,800 to complete a scheme of labourers' cottages, but
since the estimate was sent in the amount of the loan .
has been reduced to ,600. This would relieve the
ensuing year's repayment instalment of about 660.
Showing in all a reduction of C 230 in the Ardee esti-
mates, which might be allowed for in striking. the- rate,
without disimproving the original financial position.
Would you kindly bring the matter under the notice
of the Finance Committee or of the Council, which ever
body has next to consider your estimate.
Yours faithfully,
THOMAS B. DROMGOOLE,
Clerk R. D. Council.
T. F. Filgate, Esq.,
Sec. Co. Council, Dundalk.

Local Government Board, Dublin,
26th February, 1912.
Local Government Board, Dublin,
29th February, 1912.
Sir,
I am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to acknowledge the receipt of your letter of
the 19th inst., relative to the superannuation allowanqe
proposed to be granted to Mr. C. E. McCann ip respect
of his services as Collector Df Poor Rate in Louth County,
and with reference thereto, I am to point out that Sec.
10 of the Local Government (Ireland) Act, 1902, only
qualifies the provisions of Section 1 of the Local Officers
Superannuation Act (Ireland), 1869, and that the latter
Act is confined in its operation to the officers of certain
specified municipalities.
I am, sir,
Your obedient servant,
A. R. BARI-AS, Secretary.
The Secretary,
Louth County Council.
Sir,
With reference to the preparation of your estimate
to provide for the epcpenditure of the County during the
next financial year in connection with liability for land
purchase, I am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to acquaint you that they have now received
copies of certificates from the Irish Land Commission
and the Commissioners of National Debt, showing the
actual arrears in purchase annuities, &c., under the
Land Purchase Acts, and the Board are, therefore; in a
position to state definitely, for the information of the
County Council, that the amount which will be deducted
in respect of the above liability from the Estate Duty
Grant, payable in the County in the •course of the next
month, is a sum of C127 11S lid.
I am, sir,
Your obedient servant,
A. R. BARLAS, Secretary.
The Secretary,

Secretary's Office,
Court House, Dundalk,
20th March, 1911.
DEAR SIR,
I have been authorized by Requisition, (signed
by the Chairman) Io summon a Special Meeting of the
County Council for Monday next, the 25th day of March,
1912, at ONE o'clock p.m., for the purpose of considering
the undermentioned business, viz. .—
(1) Consideration of the Provisional Order for the
proposed Harbour at Annagassan, and supporting same
before the Board of Trade.
(2) To consider the advisability of appointing Dele-
gates to represent the County Council at any Con-
ventions of the United Irish League for the Divisions
of South and North Louth, which may be called within
this year.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. F. FILGATE,
Secretary County Council.
The terms of the Draft Order [or the proposed
Harbour at Annagassan having been explained to the
Council by Messrs. O'Connell and Murphy, Solicitors,
19
and the Council having thoroughly discussed the matter,
the following resolution was unanimously agreed to :
Proposed by Mr. Edward Goodman ;
Seconded by Mr. James Wynne, and
Resol ved—That the constitution of the Harbour
Commissioners should be as follows, viz. :—Three
members of the County Council ; three Rated Occupiers
resident within a radius of ftve miles of the proposed
works ; and two Rated Occupiers resident in the County
of Louth ; and further, that we nominate the following
as the first Commissioners, viz.:—Messrs. Patrick Byrne,
J.P. ; Michael Meade, and Patrick McGee, members of
the County Council ; and the following Rated Occupiers
resident within five miles of the proposed works, viz.:—
C. D. King, Joseph Hoey, Peter McGuinness ; and two
Rated Occupiers resident in the County Louth, viz.
Joseph S. Harmon, and Mathew Clinton, subject to
the Board of Trades' sanction to this alteration.
The Council were unanimous in passing the following
resolution, viz. :
Proposed by Mr. T. J. Byrne, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. C. A. Duffy, J.P., and
Resolved-—That in view of the fact that this Draft
Order is unanimously agreed upon by the Louth County
Council, the Dundalk Harbour Commissioners, and the
promoters of the proposed Works—that we, the Louth
County Council, earnestly request the Board of Trade
to sanction the Draft Provisional Order in the terms as
now agreed to.
T. F. FILGATE,
(Signed)
Secretary Louth County Council

THE COURTHOUSE, DUNDALK,
11th March, 1911.
MR. CHAIRMAN AND GENTLEMEN—I respectfully beg to
apply for some remuneration for the extra work imposed
on me through the Revision, which lasted a fortnight
last year, and for which work there was no extra pay ;
also the preparation of the two Courts for Petty
Sessions every week now, instead of one as heretofore.
The Courts have to be heated every Friday during the
winter months, which work I do.
I understand you have no power to increase my salary,
which is only CIO per annum, but my predecessor had
CIO a year for extra work, and had not nearly as
much work to do as I have, and now that matters have
considerably changed since then, might I respectfully
ask that you will grant me a similar sum to what my
predecessor enjoyed. am now nearly six years in the
Courthouse, and trust I have carried out the duties of
same to your entire satisfaction.
Thanking you in anticipation.
I am, Gentlemen, your obedient servant,
T. MURRAY, Court-keeper.

THE NATIONAL INSURANCE ACT OF 1911.
Irish County Councils General Council,
28th March, 1912.
Re COUNTY INSURANCE SOCIETIES.
Dear Sir,
Referring to my circular letter of 23rd ult.,
1
have much pleasure in forwarding for the information of
your Council the enclosed scheme for a County Society
under the National Health Insurance Act. The scheme
has been prepared by the Dublin County Solicitor, and
approved of by the Dublin County Council. The adop-
tion of a more or less uniform scheme for County
Societies would tend to greater con venience in working
the Act.
I am pleased to inform you that in view of represen-
tations made to them, the National Health Commission-
ers for Ireland have altered the date fixed for priority of
consideration of Schemes from the 30th March to the
15th April.
Yours faithfully,
A. KERAGH NOLAN, Secretary.
29
Irish County Councils General Council,
10 Leinster Street, Dublin,
February 23rd, 1912.
COUNTY INSURANCE SOCIETIES.
Dear Sir,
I am directed by the Executive Committee of
the Irish County Councils General Council to draw the
attention of your Council to the provisions contained in
the National Insurance Act for the formation of County
Health Insurance Societies by County Councils
Under Section 81 (7) of the Act it is provided that if
it appears to any County Council that having regard to
the number of employed authorities resident in the
County who are not members of any society approved of
under the provisions of the Act, it is desirable that steps
should be taken by the Council för the establishment of
an approved society under the Council, they may at any
time, before the expiration of one year from the com-
mencement of the Act, submit to the Irish Insurance
Commission a scheme for the establishment of a County
Society. The Scheme may provide for—
(a) The representation of the Council on the COID -
mittee of Management of the Society.
(b) The appointment of Officers, subject to the ap-
proval of the County Council.
(c) The delegation of powers to Committees.
(d) The giving of security by means of a charge upon
the general purposes rate or otherwise.
(e) The- restriction of membership to insured persons
resident in the County, not being members of any other
approved Society.
(f) The reduction of benefits below the minimum rates
fixed by this part of this Act ; and
(g) Such other matters as may appear necessary, and
in particular such further modifications of the provisions
Of this part of this Act, with respect to approved
Societies as may be required for the purpose of adapting
those provisions to the case of a County Society.
County Councils are further authorised at any time
after the passing of the Act, to take such steps as appear
30
necessary to ascertain what persons resident in the
County are eligible and willing to become members of
the proposed County Society, and generally for the
formation of the Society.
I am further directed to inform you that the desir-
ability of the formation of County Societies has been
considered by the Exective Cornmittee of the General
Council, who have unanimously decided to recommend
the afflliated Councils to take the necessary steps for
the formation of Schemes for their establishment, in
order that every employed " insurable person in their
area may be afforded an opportunity of participating in
the superior advantages conferred by membership of
an " Approved Society," over those enjoyed by "Deposit
Contributors."
I may point out that membership of a County Society
would be confined to residents in the county ; its funds
could not be made available to meet deficits elsewhere ;
and each claim for benefit would be dealt with by local
committees having full knowledge of the facts, so that
even the most remote districts of the county would be
provided with an effective organization.
I have to forward, later, a draft skeleton scheme for
the information of your Council.
Yours faithfully,
A. KEOGH NOLAN, Secretary.
Ordered that a copy of the foregoing correspondence
be sent to the Council's Solicitor with a request that he
furnish the Finance Cotmnittee with an explanatory re-
port and advise what action the Council should take in
the matter.

Letter from Mr. James Dromgoole as under—
Dunleer, 21st March, 1912.
Dear Sir,
In reply to your query re order mud cart, Reilly
reports that Leonard took the cart last July to supply
water to the Sheep Dipping tanks, and that one wheel
fell to pieces, after being returned, necessitating the
building of a new wheel which cost 20s.
The contractor for Ardee streets is responsible for
ordinary repairs, but now that the cart is being used for
other purposes than mud removal, I think the responsi-
bility ought to be divided.
J was present when Mr. Lynam told Reilly to report
the matter to Leonard so that he might bring the matter
before the Committee.
The Committee agreed with the suggestion of Mr.
Lynam which was that Reilly be allowed C 1 by the
Sheep Dipping Authority.
The Secretary reported that 7 of the 8 collectors had
duly lodged the full amount of their warrants in respect
of the half-year ended 31st March last, the exception
being Mr. Patrick Hughes, Park Street, Dundalk.

Committee as to his failure in not closing by the statu-
tory time, Mr. Hughes addressed the following letter to
the Committee
Park Street, Dundalk,

LOUTH COUNTY COUNCIL.

2
The following correspondence was then read :—
Francis Street, Dundalk,
15th April, 1912.
Dear Sir,
I duly received your letter of ISt inst., en-
closing letters received from the Irish County Councils
General Council with Draft Scheme of the County
Dublin County Council for the e.stablishment of a
County of Dublin Society under the Act.
With reference to the establishment of a County
Society under Section 81 (7) of the Act, I am of opinion
that it is a matter that should receive the immediate
attention of the Council. This Act is a new one, and
does not come into operation until the 15th July next,
save as otherwise expressly provided therein. It must
be remembered that as yet the membership of friendly
societies inu Ireland is distinctly limited, in fact their
operations have been chiefly, confined to England.
Probably 1,000,000 persons will be insured in Ireland.
At the present time it is doubtful if there are 100,000
members of Benefit Societies.
In order, therefore, to
avoid any difficulty in the matter of Irish insured per-
sons joining an approved Society, and lest, owing to
any such difficulty, large numbers should be forced to
become Post Office contributors, the Act provides that
a County Council may at any time beföre one year from
the 15th July, 1912; submitfto the Irish Insurance Com-
missioners a scheme for the establishment of a County
Society, where having regard to the number of em-
ployed contributors, who are not members of an
approved society, it may appear desirable to adopt this
1.
3
course. Such Scheme may provide for the objects
detailed in the letter of the County Councils General
Council.
It is eminently desirable that every employed " in-
surable person within the County may be offered an
opportunity of participating in the superior advantages
conferred by membership of an approved society" over
those enjoyed by " Deposit Contributors."
I would suggest that a Committee be appointed to
draft a Scheme to be approved by the Council, and
afterwards submitted to the Insurance Commissioners.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
On hearing this communication read, the Committee
were unanimous in recommending—That the County
Council at their meeting, to be held on Thursday next,
the 18th inst., appoint a Committee to draft a Scheme
for the County of Louth.

Mr. Elcock's Notice of Motion is as under :
ADMINISTRATIVE COUNTY OF Lowry.
THE DOGS' ACT, 1906.
Regulations for the Control o/ Dogs between Sunset and
Sunrise.
Notice is hereby given that I shall move at the next Meeting
of the County Council, to be held on the 18ti1 day of April,
1912—That the following regulations come into operation
when same shall have been submitted to and confirmed by
the Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction
for Ireland :
1. That no dog during any of the hours between sunset and
sunrise shall be allowed outside the house or adjoining
premises of the owner thereof unless such dog shall be under
the effectual control of and in the company of its owner or
some other responsible person, so as to prevent such dog
from breaking away and worrying cattle or sheep.
2. Any dog in respect of which an offence is committed
against any of these regulations may be seized and treated
as a stray dog under the powers conferred by Section 3 of the
Dogs' Act, 1906.
.—lf a dog between the hours of sunset and sunrise
Offences
is allowed outside the house or adjoining premises of the owner
and such dog shall not be in the charge of and in the company
Of the owner or some other person, and shall not be under
such control that such dog cannot break away or worry
cattle or sheep, the owner of the dog and the person (if any)
for the time being in charge thereof, and any person causing,
directing or permitting such dog to be outside such house or
premises shall each be deemed guilty of an offence against the
Act of 1894.
3. These Regulations shall apply to the entire Adminis-
trative County of Louth, and shall be in force during the
whole year.
Signed,
LUKE J. ELCOCK.

NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE COMMISSION.
24th April, 1912.
The Secretary,
LOUTH COUNTY COUNCIL.
Formation of County Insurance Committees.
Sir,
I am directed by the National Health InsUrance Com-
mission (Ireland), to inform you that they propose to appoint
Insurance Committees under Section 59 sub-section (1) and
Section 81 sub-section (8) of the National Insurance Act, 1911.
Under Clause (b) of the Section referred to, eight members
(Of whom one at least shall be a member of a local sanitary
authority and at least two shall be women) shall be appointed
by the County Council or County Borough.
Where the Council agrees to defray any part of the cost
Of sanatorium benefit, under sub-section (4) of Section 81, the
Cotnmissioners may increase the membership oi the County
Council or County Borough on the County Committee.
I am, therefore, to request that you will be good enough
to submit this question to the next meeting of your Council
and forward for the information of the Insurance Commis-
Sioners the names of the members appointed by the County
Council or County Borough, to represent it on the Insurance
Committee.
Should the Council decide to exercise its powers under
Part 11 of the Tuberculosis (Ireland) Act, 1908, the fact
Should be stated on the Memorandum attached hereto and
the names of two additional members should be forwarded
for the consideration of the Cotnmission.
I am, sir,
Your obedient servant,
JOHN HOULIHAN, Secretary.
