WOMEN'S NATIONAL HEALTH ASSOCIATION OF
IRELAND.
VICE REGAL LODGE,
Dum,1N,
May 4th, 1912.
DEAR SIR,
At the recent Annual Meeting of the Council of the Women's
National Health Association a resolution was passed tlllani-
mottsly recommending conference between the workers of
the Women's National Health Association and the Local
Health Authorities in Ireland with reference, to the National
Scheme for the suppression of Ttlberculosis about to be
inaugurated in connection with the National Health Insurance
Act.
A large proportion of the Branches of the W.N.H.A. have
taken a deep and practical interest in the anti-Tuberculosis
Campaign, encouraging their members to study the question
of the best means of prevention and cure of the insidious
disease which is so fatal to humanity. They have used the
knowledge thus gained in various ways for the benefit of
patients brought under their notice, and funds have been
13
raised to provide suitable Institutions, to maintain specially
trained Nurses, to arrange for home treatment under adequate
supervision, and to supply the necessary nourishment, clothes,
separate beds, suitable lodgings, etc.
Vour attention has doubtless been drawn to the very
interesting and important interim report just issued by the
Committee on Tuberculosis appointed to advise on the most
efficacious system of treatment in England, Scotland, Ireland
and Wales, and probably you will shortly be considering a
scheme under which you can take advantage of the assistance
provided under the Act for the treatment and ultimate ex-
termination of Tuberculosis.
The voluntary Workers of the Women's National Health
Association desire to put themselves at the disposition of the
Local Health Authorities in Ireland for the carrying out of
such schemes, and also for the establishment and maintenance
Of properly trained Nurses and Midwives who will be required
in order to make the provisions of the National Health Insur-
ance Act effective.
We therefore beg to invite you to appoint a delegate or
delegates to attend a Conference, to be held on Thursday and
Friday, May 23rd and 24th, in the Lecture Hall of Leinster
House, by kind pernmission of the Royal Dublin Society.
We hope that in any case the Medical Officer of Health
may be one of the delegates appointed.
We are authorised to state that the Local Government
Board will regard this Conference as a Central Conference
Within the meaning of the L.G.B. Order governing such
Conferences. An excerpt of this Order is appended.
The Agenda will be sent to the delegates later, but the
subjects proposed for discussion will include the different
POints raised by the report of the Special Tuberculosis Com-
mittee already mentioned, and will also include Infant
Mortality ; consideration of the Death-rate of Women in
Child-birth in Ireland ; the provision of qualified Nurses and
Midwives ; the Health of Children ; the question of Food ;
Milk ; Water ; Housing and Sanitation, as all these subjects
will be affected by the operations of the Insurance Act and the
appointment of the County Insurance Committees.
I remain, Vours very faithfully,
ISABEL ABERDEEN.
EXCERPT FROM ORDER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD OF
13th JULYS 1894.
We, the Local Government Board for Ireland do, by this
Our Order, empower the Sanitary Authorities, for the time
being, of the several Urban and Rural Sanitary Districts in
Ireland, to pay the reasonable expenses incurred by any
member or members or by the clerk in attending any such
collference or meeting as is mentioned in the said section 2,
and any reasonable expenses incurred in purchasing reports
of the proceedings of any such conference or meeting, subjecf
to the following regulations :—
(i) The expenses incurred in attending a conference or
meeting shall only be paid in respect of attendance at
a central conference or meeting open to representatives
of all Sanitary Authorities, or at a conference or meet-
ing convened for an area including the Sanitary District
front which the persons attending as representatives
are sent and held at a place distant not more than one
hundred miles from such Sanitary District.
(2) The attendance at any conference or meeting of a
member or members of, or of the clerk to, the Sanitary
Authority of any such Sanitary District shali be
expressly authorised by a resolution passed at a meeting
of the Sanitary Authority of such Sanitary District—a
written or printed notice that the proposal is to be
considered at that meeting having been sent, by post
or otherwise, to each member of the Sanitary Authority
concerned not less than four days prior to the date of
such meeting ; and where the attendance of more than
one member is authorised, the number of members
authorised to attend shall be specified in lhe resolution.
(3) In the case of a central conference or meeting open Io lhe
representatives of all Sanitary Au/,horities only one
member shall be authorised to attend from a Sanitary
District which is situated at a distance of more than fifty
miles from the Place at which such conference or meeting
may be held.
4)
15
(4) The number of copies which may be purchased by the
Sanitary Authority of any Sanitery District of the
report of a central conference or meeting, or of any
conference or meeting "for an area including the Sanitary
District of such Sanitary Authority, shall be such as
the Sanitary Authority of the Sanitary District may,
by resolution, determine.

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
At the 2nd Quarterly Meeting, May, 1912.
Mr. Chairman and Gentlemen,
The Proposal No. 1, Drogheda, for the construction of
576 perches of new road through Castlelumley, was passed
provisionally last quarter, and now comes up for final consi-
deration. Practically half the road is made already, and the
remaining half is to be metalled only twelve feet wide—still
the sum allowed, C 150, is very small.
(11.) As to No. 1, Dundalk, the footpath along the Newry
Road, from Mr. Rice's stores, near Lisdoo, to the New
Cemetery, is 204 perches in length nearly, of which about 52
perches are situated in the Urban District, and 152 perches in
the Rural District of Dundalk.
If this footpath is to be re-
paired, the usual and legal course is that each Council should
repair its own portion of the footpath, quite independently of
the other Council.
In July next the Rural Council can for-
mulate a Proposal, and set a Contract, to repair their 152
„ perches, and then there may possibly be a year or two to wait
before the Urban Council find it convenient to repair their
52 perches.
It seems that the Urban Council now intend to
complete their own 52 perches, and 25 perches in the Rural
District at an indefinite cost, with a contribution of L 50 from
the Rural District. But a Council cannot get rid of its legal
17
obligations to the public, by paying over a sum of money to
Joint works are carried out by a oint
another Council.
Board, armed with special legal powers, as iti the case o the
beg to recommend that this Proposal be
New Cemetery.
rejected, aud that, if required, a new Proposal be formulatéd
in June and July next, for the Rural District only, to extend
over two or three years if thought convenient.
I am, Gentlemen,
Your obedient servant,
P. J. LYNAM, Co. Surveyor.

Mr. T. Murray, was next read :—
THE
Mr. CHAIRMAN AND GENTLEMEN,
from the Court Keeper,
COURTHOUSE,
DUNDALK,
11th March, 1912.
I respectfully beg to apply for some remuneration for the
extra work imposed on me through the Revision, which
lasted a fortnight last year, and for which work there was no
extra pay ; also the preparation of the two Courts for Petty
Sessions every week nöw, instead of one as heretofore. The
Courts have to be heated every Friday during the winter
months, which work I do.
I understand you have no power to increase my salary,
which is only LIO per annum, but my predecessor had LIO a
year for extra work, and had not nearly as much work to do
as I have, and now that matters have considerably changed
since then, Illight I respectfully ask that you will grant me a
similar sum to what my predecessor enjoyed. I am now
liearly six years in the Courthouse, and trust I have carried
out the duties of same to your entire satisfaction.
Thanking you in anticipation,
I am, Gentlemen,
Vour obedient servant,
T. MURRAY, Court-keeper.
The Committee referred the further consideration of this
letter to the next meeting of the County Council.

• The undermentioned letter from Mr. Patrick Hughes,
Poor Rate Collector, was taken into considerati011 .
PARK STREET,
DUNDALK,
4/5/12.
GENTLEMEN,
I am in receipt of a copy of a letter of the Local Govern-
ment Board dated 14th ultimo, relating to my non-closing
collection on 31st March last.
In reply, I beg to state that ever sillce my appointment I
have found it impossible to collect the total amount of recover-
able rates in my warrant on the date fixed by the Local
Government Board without resorting to extreme measures
against many respectable ratepayers. who, if given a little
extrat ime, would discharge their obligations honourably.
However, subject to your approval, I shall relinquish the
Electoral Divisions of Ballymascanlon and Faughart and
retain Dundalk Rural, which with my duties as Collector
Dundalk Urban I shall be able to discharge in a manner
that will give no cause of complaint.
Trusting you will give my proposal your favourable con-
sideration, and thanking you for past kindness,
I am, Gentlemen,
Vours faithfully,
PATRICK HUGHES.
To the Chairman and Gentlemen Louth County Council.
The Council, having given due consideration to the fore-
going letter, were unanimous in passing the following
resolution :—

in the letter of Mr. Patrick Hughes we hereby relieve him of

and allow him to retain Dundalk Rural Electoral Division,
aud that we advertise for a Collector for the Electoral Divisions
of Ballymaseanlon and Faughart, subject to the approval
of the Local Government Board.
PETER HUGHES, Chairman.

No. 30,420, 1912, LOUTH COUNTY.
11th June, 1912.
SIR,
In reply to your letter of the 6th instant relative to the
proposal of the Louth County Council to re-arrange the
Collection District of Collector Patrick Hughes, I am directed
by the • Local Government Board for Ireland to state that
they now agree to Mr. Hughes retaining the Dundalk Electoral
Division as his Collection District.
The Board consider that it might have been more desirable
to merge the remaining District Electoral Divisions in the
adjacent Collection Districts ; but they will not object to their
being treated as a separate Collection District for the current
year, at the end of which period the arrangement will be
reconsidered.
A list of queries (in duplicate) respecting the person who
may be appointed as Collector for the new Collection District
is enclosed herewith.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
A. R. BARLAS, Secretary.

CORRESPONDENCE.
Letter from the Department of Agriculture and Technical
Instruction, as under
WEEDS AND AGRICULTURAL SEEDS (Ireland) ACT, 1909.
8th June, 1912.
SIR,
I have to acquaint you that the Department propose to
commence the work of inspection under the above Act in your
County at an early date. In this connection I have to express
28
the hope that your Council will assist the Department in this
matter as far as possible, by taking steps to assure that all
roads and lands in their control should be kept clear of harm-
ful weeds.
The Department feel that the co-operation of your Council
in this way will be appreciably reduce any difficulty which
may be experienced in inducing farmers and other occupiers
to keep their lands freed, and that the useful purposes for
which the Act was passed will be thereby greatly promoted.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.

SIR,
In reply to your letter of the 6th instant relative to the

they now agree to Mr. Hughes retaining the Dundalk Rural
District Electoral Division as his Collection District. The
Board consider that it might have been more desirable to
merge the remaining District Electoral Divisions in the
adjacent Collection Districts, but they will not object to their.
being treated as a separate Collection District for the current
year, at the end of which period the arrangement will be re-
considered.

may be appointed as Collector for the new Collection District
is enclosed herewith.
I am, &c.,

The following communication from the Local Government
Board was read by the Secretary :—
28th June, 1912.
SIR,
The Local Government Board for Ireland have had before
them your letter of the 20th instant, relative to the proposed
appointment of Mr. J. V. Johnston as Collector of Poor Rate
for the Collection District consisting of the District Electoral
Divisions of Ballymascanlon and Faughart, and with reference
thereto, they direct me to state that they observe the scale
of remuneration of the Collector has not been inserted in the
query forms (returned herewith). The Board are of opinion
that in the case of this appointment the Collector should be
paid by poundage, and not by an annual salary, and they
would be glad to learn what rate of poundage the County
Council would propose.
I am at the same time to point out that the remuneration
of Collector Hughes in respect of the District which he has
been allowed to retain has not been approved, and they
consider that in his case also payment by poundage should
be adopted, as long experience satisfies them that this method
of payment provides an incentive to expeditions collection.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN, Assistant Secretary.

NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE COMMISSION (Ireland).
25th June, 1912.
SIR,
I am directed by tlle National Health Insurance Com-
mission (Ireland) to state for the information of the Louth
County Council that they have had under consideration the
general question of the establishment of county societies for
the purposes of the National Insurance Act, 1911.
The Commission have already decided that they could not
allow more than one-fourth of the total number of members
Of the Committee of Management of such a society to be elected
by a County Council.
It has, however, been represented to
the Commission by a deputation from the General Council
Of County Councils that County Councils generally did not
regard this proportion as affording to them from their point
Of view adequate representation on the Committees of Manage-
ment, and the Commission will in the circumstances be pre-
pared to allow, subject to certain conditions, a larger pro-
Portion of the members of such committees to be elected
by County Councils.
34
It will be observed that under Section 81 (7) (b) (vi.) of the
Act, a scheme for the establishment of a County Society may
provide for the reduction of benefits below the minimum rates
fixed by Part I. The intention of the Legislature in granting
this special privilege to county societies was that such societies
might be enabled to admit to membership, without financial
risk, persons who though not quite up to the normal standard
of health could not be regarded as actually unhealthy lives,
and who would otherwise have to become deposit contributors
owing to the unwillingness which might be felt by ordinary
approved societies, bound to pay the normal benefits pre-
scribed by the Act, to admit such persons as members.
The Commission propose to have tables of reduced benefits
to be paid in respect of such persons prepared by their Actuary,
and if the County Council will agree to the principle of having
such reduced benefits incorporated in their scheme, the Com-
mission will allow the County Council to elect one-third of
the members of the Committee of Management of the county
society. It is to be borne in mind that the provision of reduced
benefits of this nature will not preclude the county society
from paying the ordinary benefits to those members whose
stateof health on admission shall be considered satisfactory.
As the preparation of the tables referred to will necessarily
involve some delay, and as it is proposed to issue a further
list Of approved societies early next week, the Commission
suggest that if the County Council decides to provide reduced
Ipenefits, the following rule should be inserted in the scheme
and the necessary changes made therein with regard to the
respective number of the members of the Committee of
Management to be elected by the Council and by the members
of the society :—
' The society will accept and comply with any scheme or
schemes giving effect to Section 81 (7) (b) (vi. and vii.)
of the Act which may be submitted by the County
Council and approved by the National Health Insur-
ance Commission (Ireland), and on such approval being
given the aforesaid scheme or schemes shall be deemed
to be incorporated in these rules, and it shall be the
duty of the Secretary to furnish every member affected
by such scheme or schemes with information regarding
same.'
95
In the event of the Council adopting a scheme of reduced
benefits it will be desirable to incorporate in the form of
application for membership some further questions, details
of which will be embodied in the scheme to be prepared by
the Commission. It will be necessary for each county society
to make its own arrangements with regard to the medical
examination of applicants cotning under the scheme of re-
duced benefits, but the acceptance of normal lives will be
unaffected. The tables of benefits will be drawn up in such
a manner as to ensure actuarially that any expenses falling
upon the society in connection with the medical examinations
referred to shall not entail any loss to the funds.
I am to add that if it is desired that the Louth County Society
should be included in the next list of approved societies,
it is essential that the Commission should be furnished by
Saturday next at latest with the decision of the County Council
and the scheme drafted in accordance with such decision,
should also be received in this Department by that date.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
JOHN HOULIHAN, Secretary.
The Secretary County Council.
Proposed by Mr. B. Hamill, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. E. Goodman, and
Resolved—That we hereby appoint all the members of the
County Council to constitute the Provisional County Society
Under the National Insurance -Act, 1911, until the election
Of the Committee of Management under the Rules. Five
members to form a quorum.
Proposed by Mr. P. O'Connell,
Seconded by Mr. William Woods, and
Resolved—That the reduction of benefits below the mini-
mum rates fixed by Section 81 (7) (b) (vi.) of the National
Insurance Act, 1911, be not adopted under the scheme for
formation of a County Society.

The following correspondence was then read :—
FRANCIS STREET,
DUNDALK,
15th April, 1912.
DEAR SIR,
I duly received your letter of ISt instant enclosing letters
received from the Irish County Councils General Council
with draft scheme of the County Dublin County Council for
the establishment of a County of Louth Society under the
Act.
With reference to the establishment of a County Society
under Section 81 (7) of the Act, I am of opinion that it is a
matter that should receive the immediate attention of the
Council. The Act is a new one and does not come into
OPeration until the 15th July next, save as otherwise expressly
provided therein. It must be remembered that as yet the
membership of Friendly Societies in Ireland is disticntly
limited ; in fact, their operations have been chiefly confined
to England. Probably 1,000,000 persons will be insured in
Ireland. At the present time it is doubtful if there are
100,000 members of benefit societies. In order, therefore,
to avoid any difficulty in the matter of Irish insured persons
joining an approved society, and lest, owing to any such
difficulty, large numbers should be forced to become Post
Office Contributors, the Act provides that a County Council
may at any time before one year from the 15th July, 1912,
submit to the Irish Insurance Commissioners a scheme for
the establishment of a County Society, where, having regard
to the number of employed contributors who are not members
of an approved society, it may appear desirable to adopt this
course. Such Scheme may provide for the objects detailed
in the letter of the County Councils General Council.
It is eminently desirable that every " employed " insurable
person within the County may be afforded an opportunity
of participating in the superior advantages conferred by
membership of an approved society over those enjoyed by
Deposit Contributors.
I would suggest that a Committee be appointed to draft
a Scheme to be approved by the Council, and afterwards
submitted to the Insurance Commissioners.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
On hearing this communication read the Committee were
unanimous in recommending—" That the County Council
at their meeting, to be held on Thursday next, the 18th inst.,
appoint a Committee to draft a Scheme for the County of
Louth."

Undermentioned letter from the Local Government Board
L.G. BOARD, DUBLIN,
16th Aftril, 1912.
SIR,
I am directed by the Local Government Board for Ireland
to state for the information of the Louth County Council
that the Board have had before them your letter of the ISt
Instant, in which you state that all the Collectors in the
County had closed their collections on the 30th ultimo, with
the exception of Collector Hughes, in whose district the large
sum of {619 43. 6d. was outstanding at the close of the-half-
Year.
40
The County Council are doubtless aware that Collector
Hughes is also Poor Rate Collector under the Dundalk Urban
District Council and closed his collection in that town within
the prescribed time.
It is therefore evident that Collector Hughes is unable to
properly discharge his duties under the two Councils, as while
he closed his collection in Dundalk Urban District, he has
been negligent in the discharge of his duties as regards the
County collection.
The County Council should, therefore, request him to forth-
with close his collection, and at the same time to inform him
that he must resign his position as a County Poor Rate
Collector or cease to act in Dundalk Urban District, and that
unless he undertakes to do this, measures will be taken to
compel him to do so.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN, Assistant Secretary.

NATIONAL HEAIÆH INSURANCE ACT, 1912.
In connection with the above the Secretary read the follow-
ing correspondence :—
DUBLIN,
24th April, 1912.
FORMATION OF COUNTY INSURANCE COMMITTEES.
SIR,
I am directed by the National Health Insurance Commission
(Ireland) to inform you that they propose to appoint Insurance
Committees under Section 59 Sub-section (1), and Section 81
Sub-section (8) of the National Insurance Act, 1911.
Under Clause. (b) of the Sub-section referred to, eight mem-
bers (of whom at least one should be a member of a Local
Sanitary Authority, and at least two should be women) shall
be appointed by the County Council or County Borough.
Where the Council agrees to defray any part of the cost of
Sanitorium benefit under Sub section (14) of Section 81,
the Commissioners may increase the Membership of the County
Council or County Borough on the County Committee.
I am, therefore, to request that you will be good enough
to submit this question to the next meeting of your Council,
and forward for the information of the Insurance Com-
missioners the names of the members appointed by the County
Council or County Borough to represent it on the Insurance
Committee.
Should the Council decide to exercise its powers under
Part 11. of the Tuberculosis (Ireland) Act, 1908, the fact
should be stated on the Memorandum attached hereto, and
the names of two additional members should be forwarded
for the consideration of the Commission.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
JOHN HOULIHAN, Secretary.

CORRESPONDENCE :
The following letter from Mr. Harry G. Tempest, for the
Hon. Sec. County Louth Archæological Society, was then
read by the Secretary
DUNDALK,
A15ril 23rd, 1912.
DEAR Mr. FILGATE,
During the last year the Archæological Society here paid
Rates to the County Council amounting in all to {5 13s. 9d.
on " Dun Dealgan," which place has been all that time
practically public property as a National Monument and
Museum. We have spent as much money as we have been
able to collect on it, and intend, if we are able, to lay out
more in making it a worthy memorial of ancient Ireland
and of the County Louth.
As you are aware, there has not been, and will not be any
profit made out of it, and as the property has now been formally
exempted from rates, the Society wish to petition for the
refund to them of the amount already paid before steps could
be taken to obtain exemption.
I should be obliged if you would kindly bring this matter
before the Finance Committee at their next meeting, and I
am sure that we may count on their acceding to our request.
Yours, &c.,
HARRY G. TEMPEST.
45
The Committee unanimously recommended thac the sum
Of {5 13s. 9d. be refunded to the County Louth Archæological
Society, provided the Local Government Board gave their
sanction to this course.
The following letter from the Secretary, Irish Automobile
Club, Dawson Street, Dublin, was then read :—
3rd May, 1912.
DEAR SIR,
The Committee of this Club has had under consideration
the fact that certain Councils in this country has made no
application to the Road Board for a Grant out of the monies
contributed by Motorists for taxes in aid of the improvement
of roads.
As it appears that no such application has been made from
Co. Louth, I am instructed to ask you to be good enough to
inform me whether any additional steam rolling is intended
to be done in your County during the present year.
Vou are, of course, aware that it is optional that motorists
should pay their annual license and also register their cars
in any County, and it is felt that those Counties which are
Prepared to take steps to improve their roads should be
Stlpported. The Committee are, however, reluctant to give
any advice to motorists on the subject, realizing that non-
Payment of their license duties in particular Counties in
addition to registering their cars elsewhere would be a consider-
able loss to sucll Counties, While their abstention from
visiting any particular Counties would be a loss to the Hotel-
keepers in these districts.
Under the circumstances, I trust you will be good enough
to let me hear from you all the information which I have
asked.
Yours faithfully,
H. S. CHAVTOR, Secretary.
The Committee then directed the Secretary to send the
following reply to the Secretary of the Automobile Club :—
LotJTH COUNTY COUNCIL.
SECRETARY'S OFFICE,
13th May, 1912.
DEAR SIR,

Automobile Club, that the Dundalk Urban Council have
expended a sum of 183 in respect of tarring and surface
repairs on their roads, while in the Rural Districts of the

for a considerable time past, and will continue to work all
year.
Vours faithfully,
T. F. FILGATE, Secretary.

in which the sewerage tanks are situated.

T. B. DROMGOOLE,
Clerk Ardee Rural District Council.
The Committee, having duly considered the terms set
forth in the foregoing letter, unanimously agreed to accept
same, subject to the approval of the County Council.

ARDEE,
31st August, 1912.
GENTLEMEN,
As you are no doubt aware the Rate Collectorship
of Ardee Urban and Rural, Clonkeen, Mansfieldstown
and Tallanstown has been rendered vacant owing to
the recent death of my father, Mr. Patrick Moonan,
who, I am sure your Council will grant, capably filled
the position for a long number of years. Having been
4
assisting him in his business and in the Collection of the
Rates, I possess an intimate knowledge of what is re-
quired to efficiently carry out the duties, and I beg to
apply -to your Council for the favour of a temporary
appointment to the vacant position to enable me to
complete the current years collection.
Thanking you in anticipation,
I am, Gentlemen,
Vour obedient servant,
JOSEPH MOON AN.

FRANCIS STREET, DUNDALK,
19th August, 1912.
Re holding MiiS MARY TOAL, Commons, Dromiskin.
i)EAk SIR,
With reference to letter of Poor Rate Collector
Cluskey of to-day's date applying for refund of L 3' 13s. 4d.
Poor Rates paid by him to 31st March last, I act for
Miss Toal and am aware of my ,owll knowledge that the
lands were let in eon-acre for the last time in May, 1010.
They hgve been vacant from November, 1910, to the
present time. Poor Rates were paid to 31st March, 1911,
by Mr. Thomas J. Byrne, Auctioneer, who acts as agent
for Miss Toal.
The holding had been let frolll year to year, and Miss
Toal finally put up the farm for sale by public auction
on the 19th Novelliber, 1909. The lands were knocked
down to Mr. Lawlor for L380, but he refused to complete
5
the purchase by reason of the threats made to him and
his wife by John Toal the Vendor's brother. Similarly
no person would further take the lands yearly by reason
of the threats of John Toal. The holding is lying
derelict, and Miss Toal is coming over from Washington
D.C., U.S.A., at the 'end -of the present month in an
endeavour to effect a sale.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
FRANCIS STREET, DUNDALK,
29th August, 1912.
Re Dr. B. A. M'GINITY's claim.
DEAR SIR,
With reference to letter of Dr. B. A. M 'Ginity enclosing
his account of fees, amounting to 130, in connection
with the case of M'Connell v. the Council, and which
letter you referred to me for a report, I now beg to state
as follows
The action was brought by the Plaintiff for damages
for alleged injuries sustained at Carlingford Pier.
Dr. M 'Ginity was retained by the Council and profession-
ally examined the Plaintiff at Newry, attending on the
late .Nlr. W. R. Rogers, Solicitor, on several occasions
reporting on the condition Of the Plaintiff, &c., and
attended on the hearing in Dublin two days, all in the
month of February, 1907, so far as I am able to ascertain.
It would appear that the Doctor was not called as a
witness, though present in Court. A verdict was given
by Mr. Justice Kenny and a jury in favour of Plaintiff.
Vour Council moved 'the Divisional Court of King's
Bench, and were successful in having the verdict set
aside. The Plaintiff appealed to the Court of Appeal,
but was unsuccessful. On those two hearings no
witnesses, of course. were required.
I find that in the Bill of Costs taxed as against the
Plaintiff the item of 121 included therein as fees as
6
medical Witness to Dr. M 'Ginity was disallowed, as he
had •not been examined. I am informed the late
Mr. Rogers, Solicitor, frequently wrote" to Dr. 2M 'Ginity
subsequent to the termination oof J the action, and re-
questing him to furnish his bill with a view topayment
bythe Council,mnd that he ineglected to do so. Tie now,
after five years; furnishes his bill, and fixes the amount
at [30.
I have given 'the matter closer attention, and have
ascertained in detail the work done by Dr. M 'Ginity
on behalf 'Of the Council, and in all the circumstances
would suggest •that a fee of Fifteen Guineas is fair
and reasonable L one to be paid him. He would be
entitled to a fee of Three Guineas and expenses for
his visit to Newry and for each day in Dublin at the
trial and to smaller fees for the various attendances
and reports made. His claini •Of 121 being disallowed
by the Taxing Master as not being properly chargeable
in a party and party Bill of Costs as against the un-
successful Plaintiff in the action, it is for the Council
to pay him what he is legally entitled to and a fee such
as the Taxing Master would have allowed.
I return herewith the letter and account submitted.
Vours faithfully;
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
The Committee were unanimous in recommending
the County Council to adopt their Solicitor's suggestion—
that is, to allow Dr. M 'Ginity a sum of L 15 15s. od, for
his services in connection with the M 'Connell case.

The following letter from the Department was read,
but the Committee made no order respecting it :
DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE AND TECHNICAL
INSTRUCTION FOR IRELAND.
(Veterinary Branch),
13, UPPER MERRION STREET,
23rd August,
DEAR SIR,
With reference to your letter of 21st instant, I have
7
to state, for • the information of the County Louth
Executive Committee under the Diseases, Of • Animals
Acts, that the restrictions ou the movement of animals
imposed by the Departme!ltts Orders in relation. to
Foot and Mouth Disease do, •not relieve sheep-owners
from their obligation under the Sheep Dipping (Irelalld)
Order to dip their sheep within the autumn period,- and
there can be no objection to such dipping being carried
out on the lauds or premises, of the. respective .owners.
In these circumstances the Department consider nit
would be inadvisable/ for your Local. Authority to
dispense with the services of the Sheep L)ipping Superin-
tendents. who• could be, very usefully employed in seeing
that . Ä)vvners duly complied with the Sheep Dipping
Regulations and in furnishing statistics as to the Iltlluber
of sheep dipped. In existing circumstances ' there is
great nece ssity for neglecting no precautionary measure
calculated to, prevent the spread of animal disease,
and the Department trust, therc fore, that your Local
Authority will reconsider their decision in this matter,
and retain the services of the Sheep Dipping Superin-
tendents for the better enforcement of, the provisions
of the Order.
I am; Sir,
Vour obedient servant.
T. V. GILL, Secrebrv.

of 1250 per annum, aud 150 allowance for clerk and office.

Kearney, Laurence M 'Keown, John F. Murphy, and
Patrick O'Connell (7).

amendment lost.

M 'Gee moved and Mr. Peter Woods seconded—That
we hereby decide that the salary to be paid to the future
County Surveyor for County Louth be {275 per annum,
and an allowance of {50 for clerk and office.

Patrick O'Connell (7).

ARDEE AND DROGHEDA DISTRICT.
MR. CHAIRMAN AND GENTLEMEN,
There were no Applications for Works by members
of the Ardee and Drogheda District Councils at July
The Ardee Council passed a sum
Quarterly Meetings.
of 1187, and Drogheda a sum of 170, to pay expenses
for Steam-rolling for a further period of about six
months.
9
These sums, in addition to the balances remaining
over from last year's Rolling will, I think, be ample to
continue the work in South Louth to about the same
extent as last year.
Steam-rolling commenced in Ardee District on 12th
January, and finished up in Drogheda on 13th July,
after working six months (about three months in each
District). Practically the whole of the stone available
has been rolled in, and the Roller is now lying idle.
Vour contract with Messrs. Eddison & Co. was for a
period of nine months or more, and as the Rolling has
been completed in six months your contractor may
claim 7s. per day for each working day the Roller is
idle during the unexpired portion of contract.
I understand Messrs. Eddison & Co. have agreed to
relinquish this claim, and allow the Roller to remain
idle until ISt October next, provided YGt1 allow them
to do next year's Rolling.
I am, gentlemen,
Vour obedient servant,
J AMES DROMGOOLE.

CORRESPONDENCE.
LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
14th August, 1912.
Collection of Rates.
am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to state that they have observed from the
returns periodically furnished to this Department
showing the progress of the •collection " of poor rate,.
that in the case of some Collectors only a. very small
percentage of the amount of their warrents is lodged
during the early portion of each half-year. In some
districts this failure on the part of the Collectors prevails
to such an extent as to cause considerable embarrass-
ment to the Council in meeting their liabilities and in
honouring the demands made upon them by other
public bodies.
The Collectors should be aware that the first moiety
of the poor rate becomes payable once the rate is made
and the demand note served, and the second moiety
on the ISt October in each year, and there can therefore
be no reason why, if the Collectors proceed in proper
time with the work of collection, they should not be in
a position to make substantial lodgments in the earlier
months of each half-year.
The Board consider that ' it would be well for the
Council to remind the Rate Collectors that it is D their
duty to collect with due diligence all moneys payable
011 account of the rate, and to take all lawful and proper
steps for enforcing the payment of any rate not duly
paid, and it should also be made clear that any Collector
who by disregard of this regulation, -fails to close his
collection within the appointed period, forfeits his right
to any remuneration.
I am, Ir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
No order was made on the foregoing letter.

The following series of resolutions were then passed

Proposed by Mr. T. J. Byrne, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. Edward Goodman, and
Resolved—That inasmuch as the existing Stand Still
Orders have proved quite ineffectual to stamp out the
present outbreak of Foot and Mouth Disease, we, the
Louth County Council, call on the Department to
relax same and allow the free transit of Cattle, Sheep
and Pigs outside a reasonable area of where the
suspected cases exist, and that we also call on the
Department and all the Irish Members of Parliament
to press upon the English Department of Agriculture
the justice of our claim to similar treatment for this
country as exists for England and Scotland.
Proposed by Mr. B. Hamill, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. D. J. Rath, and
Resolved—That we hereby adopt the draft agreement
between the Department of Agriculture and Technical
Instruction for Ireland and the Council, having reference

gassan. as submitted by the Department and in the
form as now approved cf by the Council's Solicitor.
Proposed by Mr. Patrick Donegan, J.P.,

LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
15th October, 1912.
SIR.—'I'11e Local Governillellt Board for Ireland have
had before them your letter of the 12th ultimo, relative
to the proposals of the Louth County Council in regard
to the treatment of persons ill the County suffering
from Tuberculosis ; and, in reference to YOUT inquiry
on the subject, I am to point out that the draft scheme
submitted by the County Council appears to be one for
providing treatment for tuberculous patients in the
21
County recommended by the. County Insurance Com-
mittee. It is indefinite on many essential points, and
there is no estimate of the annual expenditure to be
incurred thereunder.
The Board would suggest the appointment of a small
Committee of the County Council to elaborate a scheme
on definite lines in consulation with their Tuberculosis
Medical Officer, setting forth the proposed provision
in regard to :
(a) Central Tuberculosis Dispensary and Branch
Dispensaries.
(b) Sanatorium Beds.
(c) Observation Beds.
(d) Beds for advanced cases.
The approximate number of the foregoing beds to be
set apart for insured and non-insured persons re-
spectively, should also be stated.
I am, Sir,
Vour obenient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE COMMISSION,
DUBLIN,
23rd October, 1912.
SIR,—The National Health Insurance Commission
(Ireland) have under consideration at present the
question of the arrangements which may be made by
Insurance Committees in Ireland for the administration
of sanatorium benefit in accordance with the provisions
of the National Insurance Act, 1911 ; and, in cotlllection
with this matter, the Commission would be grateful if
you would be so good as to inform them whether your
County Council have prepared any schetlle for the
treatment of tuberculosis in pursuance of the Tubercu-
losis Prevention (Ireland) Act, 1908.
Should your Council have not as yet drawn up a scheme
22
for dealing with tuberculosis in the County, the Com-
mission, on learning of the date of the next meeting of
the Council, would be pleased •to instruct one of their
Medical Inspectors to attend the meeting and lay before
the Council a model scheme on which a suitable perma-
nent. scheme for the treatment of the disease might be
based. The Commission recognise, of course, that the
preparation of such a scheme, is a Imatter altogether
outside their province, but they are most anxious -to
give every possible assistance to County Councils in
this matter, and they trust that your Council will not
regard thei! action in any other light.
The Commission desire to add that the attendance of
their Medical Inspector would be with the concurrence
of the Local Government Board.
I am, Sif,
Vour obedient servant,
PIERCE KENT,
Assistant Secretary.
The Chairman explained that the Local Government
Board did not sanction the scheme formulated by. the
County Council on 20t11 June last. The, object of the
meeting to-day was to consider •the following draft
scheme suggested by the National Health Insurance
Commission furnished to each member of the Council—
PERMANENT SCHEME FOR THE TREATMENT OF THE
TUBERCUI,OUS POPULATION OF COUNTY LOUTH.
The local authorities throughout the country no doubt
appreciate that the inducements. which are being offered
under the Finance, Act (1911) and the Insurance Act
(1911) do not free them from responsibility in dealing
in a thorough 'manner with the grave economic problem
of Tuberculosis, bv the execrise of the powers conferred
upon them by tfie Tuberculosis Prevention (Ireland)
Act, 1908.
23
They will realise that these inducements are rather to
assist them in bringing into operation such measures as
will ensure that no case of this disease may be permitted
to go uncared for, as in this way only can adequate
provision be made for. attaining the object of all such
schemes—namely the complete eradication of Tubercu-
losis.
In the carrying out-of such measures a large proportion
of County Councils have already undertaken to strike
a penny rate, others a halfpenny rate. They have ad-
mitted that -had they, as Councils, taken action four
years ago when the power was first conferred upon them
by Act of Parliament, the total charges both for building
purposes and maintenance for the whole tuberculous
population would, of necessity, have fallen on the rates ;
but now the Treasury Grant for building purposes
provides for the capital expenditure, and in addition
a large part of the cost of maintenance is now to be
borne by the Insurance Committee and by the Treasury,
and in this way
The population may now be divided into three classes
(a) insured persons, (b) the dependants of insured persons,
and (c) those who are neither insured persons nor the
dependants of such. /
The insured persons are provided for by means of the
funds at the disposal of the Insurance Committee,
(which in County 14011th will ultimately amount to the
sum of 1814 per annum at the rate of IS. 3d. per insured
person), and if the County Council comes to an agree-
ment with the Insurance Committee to provide treat-
ment for insured persons and their dependants, the
Committee may hand over their funds to the Cotlllcil
and in so far as thc funds of the Committee are insufficient
to meet the demands of dependants, the Cotlnty Council
may come to an agreement with tlle Treasury, each
paying half of the resulting deficit. The Councy Council
will provide also for the treatement of non-insured
persons who are not 'dependants, and provision will
shortly be made whereby the Council will be relieved
of' half' this amount also.
The following data refer to the County of Louth :
Population (1911), .
Number of Insured persons ultimately
Deaths from Tuberculosis (1910)
63,665
12,733
130
Estimated number of cases of Tuberculosis existing
in the County (all classes of the disease)
1,040
One-third of the existing cases may be calculated
as representing tuberculous insured persons—
that is
346
If each ingured person is calculated as possessing on
on an average one dependant. there will be
in addition a sirnilar number representing tuber-
culous dependants
346
Finance .
Treasury Grant for capital expenditure on buildings
e.g., Sanatorium and Dispensaries—proportion
of L 120,000 distributed 011 basis of population L 1,783
Income of Insurance Committee for Sanatorium
Benefit—Is. 301. per insured person per annum {814
Any scheme of a permanent and conlprehensive
character must take into account the particulars detailed
above, and the problem reduces itself to the consideration
of the most effective way by which such facts may be
dealt witll by means of the Treasury Grant, the income
of the Insurance Committee and by a supplementary
contribution from the rates.
The treatnlent to be provided by such a scheme falls
naturally under four heads :
(1) Dispensary.
(2) Sanatorium• (Phthisis).
(3) General Hospital (Surgical Tubercul(k5is).
(4) Domiciliary or Home Treatment.
25
(1)—The Dispensary system should consist of two
essential parts (a) The Tuberculosis Medical Officer—a
whole-time Medical Officer, who will act as Superin-
tendant of the dispensary system—with nurses, (b) The
dispensary buildings, consisting of one central dis-
pensary in some accessible position in the County and
a number of branches, the location of which will depend
on the local requirements.
One whole-time nurse at LIOO and the services of four
others as part time officials at {25 each would probably
be adequate, and it is recommended that this money
should be handed over to existing nursing societies
who will agree to supply such nurses. A Caretaker and
Clerk of the central Dispensary will be required, and
may be obtained for LI per week. The cost of medicine
and appliances might be provisionally estimated at L 100.
As regards (b) it is stated in the Intorim Report of
the Tuberculosis Committee that existing houses should
where possible be obtained, the necessary alterations
being satisfactorily carried out for L250. With respect
to the branch Dispensaries it is desirable that authorities
should be approached with a view to obtaining the use
of the existing dispensary buildings, say once a fortnight
or once a week, in certain districts, on a day distinct
from the ordinary dispensary days. These arrangements
are being carried out in certain counties already.
Where provision of such Dispensaries involves building
or alteration of existing buildings, the expense will be
borne by the Capital Grant from the Treasury.
(2) The amount of the Capital Grant is L 1.783, and
if {700 be deducted tentatively for the provision of
Dispensary buildings, there remains the sum of LI ,083
for capital Sanatorium charge. If a Sanatuorim were
built with this money at the cost of 190 per bed, an
institution of 12 beds could be provided, and this would
provide adequate treatment for only 50 persons in any
It is for the County Council to
one year at the most.
decide whether they will join with some neighbouring
county in a Joint Scheme, or make permanent arrange-
ments with existing Sanatoria (where this is legally
26
possible) or borrow a sum of money sufficient to bring
the number of beds up to the requirements of the County.
(3) Arrangements will be made with general hospitals,
such as County Infirmaries or Cottage Hospitals for the
treatment of cases of Surgical Tuberculosis—such as
gland, skin, bone and' joint disease.
(4) This is a most important factor in combating the
disease, both as regards active treatment in the first
instance and in the after care of the patients when they
leave institutions and in the discovery and rescue of
contact cases in the home.
The services of the Medical Practitioners will Ilere be
available, and in addition the patients will have the
advantage of the export knowledge of the Tuberculosis
Officer and the attention. of the Nurses. In this con-
nection, on account of the difficulty of otherwise obtain-
ing adequate isolation in many of the houses, it is
recommended that a number of movable shelters should
be provided, as in this way the other members of the
family may be protected.
The Chairman invited the Deputation from the
Louth National Health Insurance Committee to express
their views on the subject.
'IShe Rev. Canon Lockett—
Ford explained the views of this Committee. The
following members—Messrs. Goodman, Wynne, I-iamill,
and Doctors O'Brien and Irwin having addressed the
meeting, it was decided that the further consideration
of the scheme be adjourned until the next meeting Of
the County Council on 14th inst.,

WATERVNDF.R,
July 30th, 1912.2
DEAR SIR,—There is a gullet which opens up into my
land at Waterunder, and this gullet is undef the control
of the County Council. On Saturday, 27th inst.,
1
missed a cne-and-a-half-year-old Bullock from my
field, which ' adjoins the gullet. After making seareh
for him on Saturday and Sunday, my man and myself
found him dead in this gullet on Monday 29th. If the
mouth of the gullet had been properly protected this
could not have occurred, and of course I could not
31
interfere to protect it, as it is the Council's property.
I must ask your Council for payment of 18, the value
of the beast.
Yours sincerely,
1). J. DREW.

FRANCIS STREET, DUNDALK,
19th August, 1912.
Re Holding, Miss Mary Toal, Commons, Dromiskin.
DEAR reference. to letter of Poor Rate
Collector Cluskey of to-day's date applying for refund
of 13 13s. 4d. Poor Rates paid by Ilitn to 31st March
last, I act for Miss Toal, and I am aware of my own
knowledge, that the lands were let in eon-acre for the
last time in May, 1910. They have been vacant from
November, 1910, to the present time. Poor Rates were
paid to 31st March, 1911, by Mr. Thomas J. Byrne,
Auctioneer, •who acts as Agent for Miss 'l'oal.
The holding had been let frotll year to year, and
Miss Toal finally put up the farm for sale. by public
auction on the. 19th November, 1909. The lands were
knocked down to Mr. Lawlor for L 380, but he refused
to complete the purchase by reason of the threats made
to him and his wife by John Toal, the Vendor's brother.
Similarly, no person would further. take the lands
yearly, by reason of the threats of John Toal.
The holding is lying derelict, and Miss Toa] is coming
over from -Washington D.C., United States, America,
at the end of the present month in an endeavour to
effect a sale.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
39
FRANCIS STREET, DUNDALK,
29th August, 1912.
Re Dr. B. A. M'GINITV's CLAIM.
DEAR reference to letter of Dr. B. A.
M 'Ginity enclosing his account of fees amounting to
130 in ccnnection with the case of M 'Connell v. The
Council, and which letter was referred to me. for a Report,
I now beg to state as follows :—The action was brought
by the Plaintiff for damages for alleged injuries sustained
at Carlingford Pier. Dr. M'Ginity was retained by the
Council, and professionally examined the Plaintiff at
Newry, attending on the late Mr. W. R. Rogers,
Solicitor, on several occasions, reporting on the con-
dition of the Plaintiff, &c., and attended on the hearing
in Dublin two days, all in the month of February, 1907,
so far as I am able to ascertain. It would appear that
the Doctor was not called as a witness: though present
in Court. A verdict was given by Mr. Justice Kenny
and a jury in favour of Plaintiff.
Vour Council moved the Divisional Court' of King'S
Bench, and were successful in having the verdict set
aside. The Plaintiff appealed to the Court ,of Appeal,
but was unsuccessful. On those two hearings no
witnesses, of course, were required.
I find that on the Bill of Costs taxed as against the
Plaintiff the item of [21 included therein as fees as
Medical witness to Dr. M 'Ginity was disallowed, as he
had not been examined.
I am informed the late Mr. Rogers, Solicitor, frequently
wrote to Dr. M'Ginity, subsequent to the termination
of the action, and reqtlesting him to furnish his bill
with a view to payment by the Council, and that he
neglected to do so. Tle now, after five years, furnishes
his bill, and fixes the amount at 130.
I have given the matter close attention. and have
ascertained in detail the work clone by Dr. M 'Ginity
on behalf of the Council, and ill all the circumstances
would suggest that a fee of Fifteen Guineas (115 15s. od.)
is a fair and reasonable one to be paid him. He would
be ent:tled to a fee of Three Guineas (13 3s. Od.) and
expenses for his visit to Newry, and for each day in
Dublin at the 'trial, and to smaller fees for the various
attendances and reports made.
His claim of 121 being disallowed by the Taxing Master
has not been properly chargeable in a Party and Party
Bill of Costs as against the unsuccessful Plaintiff in the
action, it is not for the Council to pay him what he is
legally entitled to, and a fee such as the Taxing Master
would have allowed. I return herewith the letter and
account subnlitted.
Vours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
The Committee were unanimous in reconlmending
the County Council to adopt their Solicitor's suggestion,
that is, to allow Dr. M 'Ginity a sum of L 15 15s. od. for
his services in connection with the M'Connc11 case.
Re SHEEP DIPPERS.
The following letter from the Department was read,
but the Committee made no order respecting it :—
DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE AND TECHNICAL
INSTRUCTION FOR IRELAND.
(Veterinary Branch.)
13 UPPER MERRION STREET, DUBLIN,
23rd August, 1912.
SIR,—With reference to your letter of 21st instant,
I have to state for the information of the County Loutll
Executive Committee tlllder the Diseases of Animals
Acts, that the restrictions on the Movement of Animals
imposed by the Department's Orders in relation to
Foot and Mouth Disease do not relieve Sheep Owners
41
from their obligation under the Sheep Dipping (Ireland)
Order to dip their sheep within the autumn period, and
there can be no objection to such dipping being carried
out on the lands or premises of the respective owners.
In-these circumstances, _the Department consider it
would be inadvisable for your Local Authority to dis-
pense with the services of the Sheep Dipping Superin-
tendents, who could be very usefully employed in
seeing that owners duly comply with the Sheep Dipping
Regulations, and in furnishing statistics as to the
number of sheep dipped. In existing circumstances
there is great necessity for neglecting no precautionary
measure calculated to prevent the spread of animal
disease, and the Department trust, therefore, that your
Local Authority will reconsider their decision in the
matter, and retain the services of the Sheep Dipping
Superintendents for the better enforcement of the pro-
visions of the Order.
I am, v Ir,
Vour obedient servant,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.

DUNDALK,
22nd August, 1912.

be a very fair arrangement, as llnder the present system
some of them had to lodge as much as 160 in the half-
year on such premises, and remain out of that amount
for almost three months.

our present collections.
Vour obedient servant,

DUNDALK RURAL DISTRICT SCHOOL ATTENDANCE
COMMITTEE.
The following abstract of the Minutes of Proceedings
of this Committee was read, viz. :—The advisibilitv of
appointing an Assistant School Attendance Officer.
The subject matter of this item was very fully discussed
45
by the Committee, and Laving taken into consideration
the large number of rural children who live in the im-
mediate vicinity of Dundalk and who attend school
within the Urban area, it was deemed advisable to
recommend the appointment of an Assistant School
Attendance-Offlcer.
The Committee considered that the Urban School
Attendance Officer would be the best suited for the
position, and the Secretary was instructed to ascertain
if he would accept the position if appointed, at a salary
of Five Pounds per annum, the amount the Committee
unanimously agreed to recommend.
Finally, it was proposed by J. H. M 'Ardle, Esq.,
Seconded by M. O'Meara, Esq., J.P., and
Recommended—That an Assistant School Attendance
Oflicer to the Dundalk Rural District School Attendance
Committee be appointed at a salary of Five Pounds per
annum, and that we respectfully request the sanction
, of the County Council to this recommelldation.
The Committee unanimously recommended the
County Council to adopt the resolution to appoint the
Urban School Attendance Officer as an Assistant School
Attendance Officer to supervise the Rural District
children who attend the Urban District Schools, at a
salary of Five Pounds per annum.

LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD,
25th October, 1912.
SIR,—The Local Government Board for Ireland have
had before them the replies to tlleir inquiries respecting
Mr. Charles E. M 'Cann, to whom the County Council of
50
Louth propose to grant . a tsuperannuation allowance
in •respect of his• services as Collector of Poor Rate ill
the County ; and with reference to the reply to query
No, IOS in which the County Council state that
Mr, M 'Canu did not devote his whole time to the service
of the County, the Board desire to call attention to the
terms •of the Unioil Officers (Ireland) Superannuation
Act, 1865, as adopted by Secticn 83 of the Local Govern-
nyent (Ireland) Act, 1898, which provides that the
Cotllity Council may, with the consent of the Board,
grant a pension to any officer whose whole time has been
devoted to the services of the County.
It appears from the records of this Department, that
Mr. M 'Cann, prior to the passing of the Local Govern-
ment (Ireland) Act, 1898, held the office of Poor Rate
Collector under the Board of Guardians of Drogheda
Union, and that he was by that Act transferred as an
existing officer to the Louth County Council and other
public bodies in his capacity of Poor Rate Collector.
In these circumstances the Board would be glad to
be furnished with the further observations of the County
Council, as to whether in their opinion Mr. M'Cann
devoted his whole time to the duties of Poor Rate
Collector. The form of queries is returned herewith
for amendment, if necessary. With regard to the
amount of his superannuation allowance, the Board
desires to point Out thåt on his actual services in the
County Mr. M'Can11 could be granted a pension of
L 16 15s. 9d. a year. With the addition of seven years
he could be allowed 122 19s. 5d. Alld with ten years
added 125 12s. 5d. a year. The Local Government

ARDEE UNION,
16th October, 1912.
Dunany Pier.
DEAR SIR,—The Ardee No. 1 Rural District Council
have directed me to say fthat they have had before them
a copy of a letter addressed by the Department of
Agriculture, etc., to the County Council. offering to give
a sum not exceeding {250 towards the improvement
of Dunany Pier, provided an equal sum is forthcoming
'IShe Council consider that there is
from local sources.
no pressing necessity for this improvement, and that
as the rates are already so high and the condition of
agriculture here so unfavourable this year, the public
54
could hardly bear such an addition to local taxation,
and they think the matter could afford to lie over for
consideration twelve months hence.
THOMAS B. DROMGOOLE.
The Committee, having regard to the number of large
ratepayers in the district who signed the memorial
requesting the Department to repair the Peir, and the
Department's offer to give a grant of 1250 towards the
repair, are of opinion that the Ardee No. 1 Rural
District Council should take advantage of the Depart-
ment's generous offer and have the Pier repaired forth-
with.

COUNTY COUNCIL
COUNTY HALL, TRALEE,
25th Octobei, iöl±.
(I) Deductions /rom County Council Gr_ants (Local Taxation
Account) ; and (2) Land Purchase I.osses.
DEAR have again to respectfully direct attention to
the fact that the i' Imperial and Local •raxation Committee,"
which was appointed in April, 1911,' has; not yet submitted fits
reperto and a AvttU ,tq. hand from the Seggtary statey .thag
58
he regrets he is unable to give any information as to when his
Committee is likely to report." As the Government, meantime,
declines to make good the deductions from our grants, it is
obvious that this Committee, which is sitting" for such a consider-
able period, should be required to report without /urthev delay.
steps should then be taken to place the " Local
Taxation Account " in a solvent position, and a refund at the
same time secured of the deductions already made from our
grants. These deductions in Kerry have amounted to 15,062,
made up of Asylum, Railway and Harbour, Medical Charities,
and - other grantS.
This. amount, should—tate, independent of the Land
Purchase Losses, .which are now going on, for several years, and
have amounted in this countyl after allowing for a trifling refund,
to 15,671, and as to which, I previously stated, 'Y that thousandS
of ppunds. were withheld, no particulars worth having supplied,
no money, with a trifling exception, refunded, and no audit
authorised on behalf of the Councils concerned." More time
should also be given for the collection Of these annuities, in the
manner previously suggested, before any deductions are made
from our grants
The Home Rule Bill, which standari.ses the grants to be
transferred to the Irish Government at the passing of the Act,
at the amounts actually PAID at the time by the Treasury to
the Irish Local Authorities, as distinct from the amounts due,
makes the question of those grants a matter of additional im-
portance, if •the Irish Ratepayers are not to be required to meet
liabilities morally and lawfully due by the British Treasury.
In this respect 1 previously stated that " to repair the serious
neglect of the past, which is so sadly apparent on the face and
the intellect of Ireland, to rebuild this Nation, and, by meeting
the new and growing demands of advancing civilization, to keep
it anywhere placed in the race of progress, will need all the
money at the disposal of the Irish Government without having,
at the same time, to make good losses or deductions—and
substantial and growing ones—which should be provided for-
out of the Imperial Treasury."
As the time is arriving for, I fear, further annual deductions,'
as well as for preparing our Rate Estimates Ior the coming year,
may I again respectfully direct immediate attention to this
important matter, and once more suggest that the " Imperial
and Local Taxation Committee " be called on, in Parliament,
to report, without further delay, and that the necessary steps be
taken generally to protect the, Irish Ratepayers interests while
there is yet time.
Your obedient servant,
P. M. QUINLAN, County Secretary.
To each Irish Member of Parliament.

'IN. NOi,ANl ISeéÉétåi9.
COVNTVSC110LAkSfi1PS.
FROM PR\MARV TO INTERMEDIATE SCti00LS.
Report of the Standing Committee on Legislation on the
conditions attached to a draft Stheme for -Scholarships
tenable at (ntermeciiate Schools by boys and girls _/rom
Primary Schools to be held in connection with Scholar-
shifts at Irish Universities.
Whilst we believe that Ireland owes a deep debt of
gratitude to the Right Hon. Augustine Birrell, P.C.,
Chief: Sécrétary; 'for 'his; successftil efforts -tot provide a
v system r Of' higher jeducationm acceptablec _ to, . the: great
majority of •thevlrish people, v and' whilst: we- stiggest
that the thanks bfÄhe Council] arer (Itie ,to: him 'for LI)is
further effort ttv meet 'Of the : Council by
providing •from Imperial' funds n sum- per
eannum for the' purpose- of foundillg a system1H)f Scholar-
ships from' Piitnary to 'Intermediate Schools, , the con-
• ditions attached to this; proposed Arrant, are such that
'having given themøour; most- earnest and :careiul
66
consideration, we are Compelled very reluctantly but
unhesitatingly to recommend the Councils to refuse to
avail themselves of any grant associated with conditions
of this nature, which in our opinion are highly objection-
able from many points of view.
In the first place, the acceptance Of the conditions
sought to be imposed would utterly vitiate and render
negatory an important agreement entered into between
the Senate of the National University and a deputation
from the General Council, whereby the Senate agreed
accede to the request of the deputation • that Irish
should be an essential subject for Matriculation in the
University as from 1913, and the Councils represented
agreed to strike a rate in aid of University Education
and tos found Scholarships tenable in, the National
University, the mandate of the deputation being con-
tained in a resolution adopted with but one dissentient
vote at ran important meeting of the General Council,
held on 2nd June, 1909, this resolution being founded
upon nunterous representations received from the
individual Councils forming the association, twenty-
four of which Councils had bound themselves by
resolution to strike no rate save on the above terms.
This is clearly an agreement. which should not be
lightly disregarded or set aside, aud it, is equally clear
that the Councils cannot look to the Senate for its
adhere honourably to their side of the bargain, and to
resist all inducements to violate it.
To open County Council Scholarships to any Univer-
sity which fails uto comply with the vital .condition as to
essential Irish would . manifestly . be „unfair treatment
of the University which has agreed to accept it.
It has been alleged in some quarters that in confining
their Scholarships to the National University the Irish
County Councils. whilst within their strict legal right
have acted in a narrowminded and illiberal spirit. ' We
deny that there is the smallest justifieation for this
contention, and we invite reference to facts.
The Irish Universities Act, 1908, gives, to the elected
represelltatives of the ratepayers, whose money provides
the County Scholarships, effective representation upon
67
the governing bodies of the Constituent College of the
National University and of thc National University
only. In pursuance of tlle powers thus conferred, the
Council will, before the close of thé present year, proceed
to elect nine members 'Ofl the Governing body' of Uni-
ver -tity College, Dublin, fi+e members of the Governing
body of University College, Cork, and six members of
the Governing body; : of University College, Galway:
The ratepayers -possess no Such safeguard with regard
to the' wise• and prudent expenditure of their money
ill relati011' to' any other Tjilivérsity-in Ireland, and in
ccntining their support to the University Over which
they possess some degree of control, the Councils buq
follow the example set tllem by many Corporate' and
other authorities in Great Britain, who liberally sub-
sidise Universities upon thc Governing bodies of which
they possess representation.
The Act manifestly contemplates assistance from the
County Councils supplementary to its; parsimonious
financial provision for the Universities it creates, and it
would be highly inequitable to expect the Guardians
of the ratepayers interests to add to this burden by
subsidies to already wealthy and highly endowed
Institutions.
The constitution of the National University of Ireland
has been framed by its authors with thc most scrupulous
care upon absolutely non:sectarian lines. All possi-
bility of religious preference of any description hag beeri
wholly eliminated. Thc principle' of proportional re-
presentation has been fully observed in the composition
of the Senate and of the Professional Staff, and any
objections by an Irish Student winning a County Council
Scholarship to pursue his course of studies Din this
University would be based 110t upon ieason, but upon
prejudice.
In exercising the power of election c0nferred by the
Charter, we strongly urge that the General Council
should carefully select the best qualified members of the
Council as their representative 011 the Governing Body
of University College, L)ublin, with view to retainillg
in our own hands intinlate association with the develop-
ment and progress of the University.
'68
We regard as .the- proposal to
award 'to children of tender years upon. tile, results of
an examination based chiefly upon personal estimates
of theiv natural intelligence Scholavship:; tenable at Inter-
mediåteQ Seh001Å 'linked ' with ' the extraordinary con-
ditjonfthat •at theyconclusion: of, a_ three years' •course
they are: to possess, Subject •onlyfto thé ,vetov •of, their
op.n preceptors„ an inalienable, rightÄo demand from
thé' Of theif Cotlllffåhe proviliöu of L 240 of the
ratepayers the •cost -'of •an University
Course, subject. to' 004 control, •bythe: Council tint its
award or expenditure, subject to 110 examination to
determine whether the original estimate of the child's
intelli+ency• has beep . fully justified, or whether the
youth si •préséilt intelligence and acquirements point
to a material advautageto be derived by him from Uni-
versity Education and conditional only tlpon a mere
pass. matriculation.
Viewed from a financial standpoint the proposals
are equally unsatisfactory. , For each 110,000 of free
grants, the eCouncils liltl.st covenallt. to jprovide 120,000
and to hand this sum cver for expenditure upon Scholar-
ships allocated upon a flat rate •scale vastly more
expensive . than the County Councils .own„ carefully
prepared Scholarship Scheme, framed with a view to
encourage, study in scientific agriculture and, other
stibjecté of vital iinporfance to Ireland. In facf',' it is
quite . demonstrable that 'ther County Councils coQIId

J,0CAL GOVERNMENT BOARD,
I)IJRLJN,
8th November, 1912.
SIR,—With reference to your application to the Louth
County Council for the position of County Surveyor,
I am directed by the Local Government Board for
Ireland to inform you that you are ineligible for such
appointment, as you are over the prescribed maximum
age limit of 45 years.
I am, Ir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN, Assistant Sec.
J. M. •C. LYONS, Esq.,
County Surveyor's Office,
Court House, Dundalk.
